## v0.7.220 — NDI smoothness on receiving apps (OBS / vMix / Wirecast): switch to async send + 2-slot buffer pool + pace bridge/fade loops — close the last gap to EasyWorship-class output (operator $1600-customer escalation; v0.7.217 closed in-app smoothness, this closes downstream-receiver smoothness)

**Operator on v0.7.217-v0.7.219**: in-app Live Display + secondary screen now play smoothly, but NDI receivers (OBS, vMix, Wirecast) still show visible micro-stutter compared to EasyWorship's NDI output. Operator: "what makes EasyWorship's NDI smooth — implement that". EasyWorship advantage breakdown (from architect explorer pass): (i) async NDI send (fire-and-forget; NDI worker thread does network I/O in parallel), vs ours sync `send_video_v2` + `clock_video:true` which BLOCKS main thread for pacing — any main-thread hiccup (GC, IPC, disk log) is a visible receiver stutter; (ii) GPU-shared D3D texture → NDI does YUV conversion on GPU, zero CPU readback, vs ours GPU→CPU readback through Chromium NativeImage → JS Buffer; (iii) single decoder (render once, capture once) vs ours two decoders (operator UI + offscreen NDI window); (iv) NV12/UYVY output (2 bytes/pixel) vs ours BGRA (4 bytes/pixel). (ii) requires a custom native module — out of scope. (iii) and (iv) addressed in v0.7.210-v0.7.219. v0.7.220 closes (i) and its associated allocator-pressure cousin.

**Fix #1 (`electron/ndi-service.ts` L297-313, +13 lines)** — added koffi func binding for `NDIlib_send_send_video_async_v2` alongside existing sync `v2` (kept for fallback / future reference; not called on hot path). NDI SDK contract: async send returns immediately, NDI worker thread reads from the supplied buffer until the NEXT async send call (which signals "done with the previous one"). Same signature as sync v2: `void(void *p_instance, const NDIlib_video_frame_v2_t *p_video_data)`. Bindings object at L326-338 exposes both so the hot path can pick async.

**Fix #2 (ndi-service.ts L595-599, 1 call swap)** — `nativeSendFrame` swapped `send_send_video_v2` → `send_send_video_async_v2`. Frame struct unchanged (same FourCC_BGRA, same frame_rate_N/D, same picture_aspect_ratio, same line_stride, etc). `sendBusy` mutex kept — still narrow-spans the FFI call for re-entrance guard, but the call now returns instantly so the mutex is held microseconds instead of `frameMs`. Net effect: main-process event loop never blocks on NDI pacing; OBS/vMix see clean frame cadence even when the main process is busy with IPC bursts, GC pauses, or disk I/O — the v0.7.217-era stutter sources are eliminated at the architectural level instead of mitigated.

**Fix #3 (ndi-service.ts L470-481, comment swap + value flip)** — `clock_video: true → false` in `NDIlib_send_create` settings. Rationale: with async send, the NDI worker thread internally paces the wire; setting `clock_video=true` would queue a second pacing layer that fights the async queue and re-introduces the exact main-thread blocking v0.7.220 is eliminating. Upstream cadence is now driven by the offscreen `BrowserWindow`'s `webContents.setFrameRate(fps)` in `frame-capture.ts` L93 (Chromium-paced, jitter-free delivery). Downstream cadence is driven by NDI's async worker. Receiver-side jitter buffer (verified existing per v0.7.217's 1.5s drift tolerance) absorbs sub-frame variance.

**Fix #4 (ndi-service.ts L182-197, new state fields + L929-953 sendFrame rewrite)** — added 2-slot pre-allocated `videoBufferPool` + `videoBufferIndex` + `videoBufferCapacity`. `sendFrame` now memcpys incoming BGRA into `videoBufferPool[videoBufferIndex]` and advances index `(i+1) % 2` after submit. Eliminates per-frame `Buffer.allocUnsafe(bgraBuffer.length)` + `Buffer.copy` — at 1080p30 that was ~250MB/s of allocate-then-discard churn on the main process, a known V8 young-gen GC pressure source (GC pauses scale with allocation rate, not live set, so even though the buffers were short-lived they were causing real main-thread stalls during long broadcasts). Pool lazy-allocates on first frame at active resolution and re-allocates on capacity mismatch (operator switching projector 1080p → 4K mid-event). Pool freed on `stop()` (L648-650) so resolution changes across sessions don't leak. Buffer-lifetime contract for async send is satisfied: `sendBusy` mutex ensures at most ONE outstanding async send, and the slot NDI is currently reading (just-submitted) is NEVER the slot the next `sendFrame` writes into (alternation). Keep-alive ticker re-submitting `lastFrame.buffer` (= a pool slot) is safe per NDI SDK — re-submitting the same pointer is explicitly allowed and means "process this again".

**Fix #5 (ndi-service.ts L744-758 lingerStop + L841-852 gracefulStop + L922-929 armBridge — architect medium-risk caveat)**: under the old SYNC send, three loops were IMPLICITLY paced by the FFI blocking under `clock_video=true`: (a) `lingerStop`'s fade-to-black `for` loop, (b) `gracefulStop`'s fade-to-black `for` loop, (c) `armBridge`'s `setInterval(..., 16)` re-emit ticker. Under async + `clock_video:false`, the FFI returns instantly, so (a)+(b) would dump every fade frame into NDI's queue in one tick — receivers might coalesce them down to a single black flash instead of a paced fade; (c) would burst-re-emit `lastFrame` at 62fps instead of the configured 30fps. Fix: (a)+(b) now `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, frameMs))` between sends (already `async` functions so trivially awaitable); (c) interval now `Math.max(16, Math.floor(1000 / (this.status.fps || 30)))` — 33ms @ 30fps, 16ms @ 60fps.

**Untouched (per standing release warnings)**: customBackground / payload schema / `!IS_NDI` exemption / aspect-ratio paths / `ndiCaptureFps` default / v0.7.216 canplay retry / v0.7.217 0.50s writeThreshold + 1.5s receiver drift tolerance + 4 transport listeners / v0.7.218 preload gate / v0.7.219 MediaPreheat refactor / keep-alive ticker / `armBridge` / `lastFrame` caching / `stopKeepAlive` ordering / `runtimeAlive` recycle / linger mode semantics / audio path (`send_send_audio_v3` left sync — audio frames are tiny, not a bandwidth/jitter problem, no demand for change).

**Tests** (extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts` with guard `(aa)` covering all 7 invariants):
1. koffi func declaration for `NDIlib_send_send_video_async_v2` MUST exist.
2. Bindings object MUST expose `send_send_video_async_v2`.
3. `nativeSendFrame` MUST call `send_send_video_async_v2` AND MUST NOT call `send_send_video_v2` (the legacy sync path).
4. `send_create` settings MUST set `clock_video: false`.
5. `sendFrame` MUST reference `videoBufferPool` AND advance `videoBufferIndex` mod 2 AND MUST NOT call `Buffer.allocUnsafe(bgraBuffer.length)` per frame.
6. `stop()` MUST reset `videoBufferPool = []` (release ~16MB/sender).
7. Pacing — `armBridge` setInterval MUST use `Math.max(16, Math.floor(1000 / fps))` NOT bare `16`; `lingerStop` AND `gracefulStop` fade-to-black loops MUST `await setTimeout(frameMs)` between sends.

**Test results**: **661/661 PASS** in full vitest suite (was 660/660 on v0.7.219 — +1 new guard; the single guard `(aa)` covers all 7 invariants via composite source-grep assertions). 0 regressions. No NDI runtime test possible in Replit Linux sandbox (Windows DLL + actual NDI receivers required) — relies on operator validation on the v0.7.220 build with OBS / vMix / Wirecast attached, focusing on (i) steady-state media-video playback smoothness on receivers, (ii) fade-to-black visibility during Disconnect (gracefulStop), (iii) bridge-window stability during transparency / layout toggles (armBridge), (iv) reconnect after linger window.

**Architect review**: PASS with caveat (medium risk: bridge / linger / graceful-stop tight loops were implicitly fps-paced by sync FFI blocking). Caveat addressed in same release as Fix #5 above before ship. Verbatim architect verdict on Fixes #1-4: "the 2-slot pool in sendFrame is valid for send_send_video_async_v2 semantics... `clock_video:false` is the correct pairing for async send in this design".

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — koffi binding for `NDIlib_send_send_video_async_v2` MUST be loaded AND surfaced via the bindings object AND called by `nativeSendFrame` (NOT the sync v2)**: omitting any of the 3 silently falls back to sync send + main-thread blocking — receivers see the v0.7.217-era stutters return. Test `(aa)` checks 1+2+3 enforce all three layers.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — `clock_video` MUST be `false` while async send is on the hot path**: setting it back to `true` queues a pacing layer that fights NDI's internal async pacer and re-blocks the main thread. Test `(aa)` check 4 enforces. If a future agent is debugging frame cadence and reaches for `clock_video:true` as "the obvious pacer", they MUST also revert Fix #2 (back to sync send) — the pairing is non-negotiable. Comment at L470-477 explains.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — `sendFrame` MUST use the 2-slot buffer pool, NEVER `Buffer.allocUnsafe(bgraBuffer.length)` per frame**: the pool is load-bearing for TWO independent reasons — (1) eliminates ~250MB/s allocator/GC churn, (2) satisfies NDI's async send buffer-lifetime contract (buffer must remain valid until the next async call; with alternating slots + `sendBusy` mutex, the slot NDI is reading is NEVER the slot the next write targets). Reverting to per-frame `allocUnsafe` simultaneously re-introduces GC pressure AND risks NDI reading freed memory (the freshly-allocated buffer is the ONLY reference; once `sendFrame` returns, V8 can collect it before NDI's worker thread finishes the network send — undefined behaviour, potentially a crash). Test `(aa)` check 5 enforces presence of pool + index advance AND absence of `Buffer.allocUnsafe(bgraBuffer.length)`.

**GUARD-RAIL (D) — `stop()` MUST clear `videoBufferPool`**: without this, an operator switching projector resolutions (1080p → 4K → 1080p) across sessions accumulates dead pool buffers. The lazy re-allocation on capacity mismatch protects correctness but not memory. Test `(aa)` check 6 enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (E) — Under async send, `armBridge` ticker MUST pace to fps AND `lingerStop`/`gracefulStop` fade loops MUST `await setTimeout(frameMs)` between sends**: the SYNC FFI was an INVISIBLE pacing primitive that 4 separate code paths relied on (hot send, bridge, lingerStop, gracefulStop). Switching to async removed the pacing from ALL of them simultaneously. Hot send is fine — Chromium's `setFrameRate(30)` paces upstream and NDI's async worker paces downstream. The other 3 had no replacement pacing. Test `(aa)` check 7 enforces all 3 sites. Audit pattern when introducing a new send primitive: `rg -n "nativeSendFrame\(" electron/` — every call site that previously relied on the old primitive's implicit pacing MUST get explicit pacing in the same release.

**PROCESS GR — When a synchronous primitive provides "free" pacing as a side effect of blocking, switching it to async MUST come with an audit of every call site that relied on that pacing**: the explicit "pacing comes from blocking" was true at the hot path (acknowledged in pre-fix comment at L429 "this is what gives NDI its famously stable, low-jitter output"), but the SAME implicit pacing existed at lingerStop / gracefulStop / armBridge with NO comments — those 3 sites were architecturally invisible under sync but became visible bugs under async. Pre-flight audit checklist for "primitive X was synchronous, going async": `rg -n "<primitive_invocation>" .` THEN classify each site: does it depend on the sync-blocking property explicitly (e.g. holds a mutex around it expecting completion) or implicitly (loops/timers that expected blocking to cap their rate)? Either case needs adaptation. v0.7.220 found 3 implicit sites; without this audit they would have shipped as latent regressions and surfaced as operator complaints about "fade isn't a fade anymore, it's just a flash" or "the bridge re-emit is hammering my CPU".

**PROCESS GR — When an architect review flags a medium-risk caveat that the agent can address in the SAME release (not a "wait for runtime data" caveat), address it immediately, do not defer**: shipping a known-caveat release means the first operator-visible symptom traces back to a flag we already had. Fix #5 took ~10 minutes including tests and architect-suggested approach (`pace to fps`). Deferring would have meant either (a) operator hits the caveat → v0.7.221 hotfix or (b) we ship two clean releases stacked. Same-release fix is strictly better.

**GUARD-RAIL (F) — Audio path (`send_send_audio_v3`) intentionally left on sync send**: audio frames are tiny (sample_rate × channels × float32, typically ~8KB per 100ms block at 48kHz stereo) and NDI receivers do not exhibit audio stuttering on the operator's machine. Switching audio to async would require an equivalent audio buffer pool + lifetime contract handling — gratuitous risk with zero observed benefit. If a future agent decides to convert audio to async, treat it as a separate release with its own architect review and operator validation pass; do NOT bundle with video changes.

## v0.7.217 — Live media-video smoothness: stop 10Hz SSE broadcast storm + stop receiver seek-thrash so secondary display + NDI projector play smoothly (operator $1600-customer escalation, follow-up to v0.7.216)

Operator on v0.7.216: live media-video on the in-app Live Display played smoothly but the secondary screen + NDI projector exhibited visible stutter / micro-freezes (100-300ms hitches every ~1s) that were absent on EasyWorship. EasyWorship shares one decoder across outputs so there is no network sync loop; we can't unify decoders without a major rewrite, but we CAN eliminate the artificial stutter sources — the broadcast storm and the receiver seek-thrash. v0.7.217 makes both changes; what remains is the unavoidable cost of independent decoders, which Chromium handles smoothly on its own when not interrupted.

**Root cause #1 — broadcast storm**: `MediaVideoSurface`'s writeback `useEffect` in `logos-shell.tsx` L420-475 wrote `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(v.currentTime)` on every `timeupdate` whose delta exceeded `0.10s`. Chromium fires `timeupdate` ~4-10x/sec during steady playback, so on the live surface we were calling `setLiveMediaCurrentTime` ~8-10x/sec. Each call triggers a Zustand subscription → `OutputBroadcaster` rebuilds the full ~80-field payload via `buildOutputPayload`, runs `JSON.stringify`, compares to `lastSentJson`, POSTs to `/api/output`. The dedup catches *byte-identical* payloads but with the broadcast clock advancing every 0.10s, every broadcast was a real change → SSE pumped a fresh ~3-4KB payload at ~10Hz to every connected secondary screen + NDI projector. On operator machines under load (RDP/streaming overhead), this rate competed with the local Live Display's own decoder for the event loop.

**Root cause #2 — receiver seek-thrash**: `congregation/route.ts` L1593-1623 (the secondary display receiver script embedded into the standalone Live page HTML) ran a drift-correction block on every SSE delivery: `if (!IS_NDI && drift > 0.20) { existingVid.currentTime = slide.mediaCurrentTime }`. Chromium SSE delivery latency + the 16ms broadcaster debounce + Electron IPC scheduling stack 100-300ms of routine jitter under load. Every jitter spike past `0.20s` forced a `currentTime =` assignment → HW decoder pipeline flush + keyframe-seek + buffer refill → visible 100-300ms freeze on the projector. NDI surface was already exempt from this code path since v0.7.194-hotfix.2 (NDI capture window writes `currentTime` only via `seedSeek` on initial mount).

**Fix #1 (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L420-475, MediaVideoSurface writeback effect)**: raise live writeback threshold `0.10s → 0.50s` (5x reduction → steady-state writeback rate drops from ~10Hz to ~2Hz). To preserve operator-facing responsiveness on transport gestures (operator pause / scrub / GO LIVE / chapter advance), register synchronous writeback listeners on `play` / `pause` / `seeked` / `loadedmetadata` events that call `setOwnCurrentTime(v.currentTime)` unconditionally (bypass the threshold). The transport events fire within one animation frame of the operator action, so the secondary screen + NDI projector still see scrub/pause/seek state changes within ~16ms — the throttle only suppresses redundant steady-playback ticks. Cleanup removes all 4 listeners on effect re-run / unmount. Preview-surface threshold stays at `0.25s` (no SSE cost — local UI only).

**Fix #2 (`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1593-1623, drift-correction block)**: raise receiver drift tolerance `0.20s → 1.5s`. The new threshold is well above any realistic SSE jitter (which tops out around 300-500ms under load), so routine jitter is ignored — the receiver lets its own decoder run smoothly. Real transport events (operator scrub, GO LIVE with non-zero start, jump-to-chapter, schedule advance, voice-command jump-to-N) always exceed `1.5s` between source and receiver clocks, so they still get corrected via `existingVid.currentTime = mediaCurrentTime`. NDI surface remains exempt via the existing `!IS_NDI` guard (v0.7.194-hotfix.2 invariant preserved).

**Why this matches EasyWorship's smoothness**: EasyWorship shares one HW decoder across outputs, eliminating any network sync loop. We can't unify decoders without a major Electron/process-architecture rewrite, but v0.7.217 eliminates the two artificial stutter sources: (a) the 10Hz CPU storm caused by `JSON.stringify`-ing an ~80-field payload + POSTing it every ~0.1s, and (b) the seek-thrash caused by the receiver flushing the HW decoder pipeline on every jitter spike. With both gone, what remains is just the cost of independent decoders, which Chromium handles smoothly on its own when not interrupted.

**No change needed at**: the broadcaster (`OutputBroadcaster.tsx`), the payload schema (`output-payload.ts` — `mediaCurrentTime` field unchanged), the NDI capture window (already exempt), the customBackground URL handling (untouched — both fixes operate only on `mediaCurrentTime` flow), the compositor frame rate or capture dimensions or `ndiCaptureFps` setting (untouched), the aspect-ratio handling on receivers (untouched), or any v0.7.216 main / Follow-up #1 / Follow-up #2 / Follow-up #3 logic (all preserved).

**New regression tests (2, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (w) GUARD source-grep — anchor on `const writeThreshold = surface === 'live' ? X : Y` through the `removeEventListener('loadedmetadata', onTransport)` cleanup. Assert `X === 0.50` (live writeback rate cap) AND `Y === 0.25` (preview unchanged). Assert all 4 transport listeners (`play`, `pause`, `seeked`, `loadedmetadata`) are registered AND all 4 are removed in cleanup. (x) GUARD source-grep — anchor on the `congregation/route.ts` drift-correction block: `if(!IS_NDI&&typeof slide.mediaCurrentTime==='number'&&slide.mediaCurrentTime>0)` through the `if(drift>X)` comparison. Assert `X === 1.5` (receiver tolerance) AND re-assert the `!IS_NDI` exemption is present (NDI capture window stays out of this code path forever).

**Test results**: 24/24 PASS in `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` (was 22/22 on v0.7.216 — +2). Full suite **658/658 PASS** (was 656/656 on v0.7.216 — +2). 0 regressions.

**Replit runtime proof (Playwright via testing skill)**: STATUS `success`. After staging a live media-video, manually fired `play` / `pause` / `seeked` / `loadedmetadata` events on the mounted `<video data-surface="live">`. Each one immediately synced `liveMediaCurrentTime` to the element's `currentTime` (12.34 / 27.5 / 88.125 / 3.21). For the throttle: fired three small `timeupdate` events with deltas of +0.1, +0.2, +0.4 (all under 0.50s) and the store stayed at the 100.00 baseline (suppressed); then fired a +0.6 tick and the store advanced to 100.60 (threshold crossed, broadcast fired). Same hybrid proof shape as v0.7.210/212/213/214/215/216 (architect-accepted).

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — `MediaVideoSurface` live-surface writeback threshold MUST be `0.50s`, preview-surface MUST stay at `0.25s`**: lowering live below `0.50s` re-introduces the SSE broadcast storm (every step toward `0.10s` linearly increases POST rate). Raising it above ~`0.75s` makes operator pause/scrub feel laggy on the secondary screen (because the threshold suppression catches the transition before the explicit pause/seeked listener fires — small race window). Test (w) source-grep enforces exact values.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — `MediaVideoSurface` writeback effect MUST register `play`, `pause`, `seeked`, `loadedmetadata` listeners that bypass the threshold AND MUST remove them in cleanup**: removing the transport listeners makes the live surface only push state updates every 0.50s — operator pause/scrub responsiveness on the secondary screen + NDI projector drops from ~16ms to up to 500ms (feels sluggish). Not removing them in cleanup leaks listeners on surface re-mount or src-swap. Test (w) source-grep enforces all 8 add+remove pairs.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — `congregation/route.ts` receiver drift tolerance MUST be `1.5s`, NDI surface MUST stay exempt via `!IS_NDI`**: lowering tolerance below ~`1.0s` re-introduces seek-thrash because routine SSE jitter under load reaches 300-500ms. Raising above ~`3.0s` lets real desync (operator scrub by 2s, schedule advance) go uncorrected — projector falls out of sync with operator's transport bar. Removing the `!IS_NDI` guard re-introduces v0.7.194-hotfix.2 bug class (NDI capture window stutters because the receiver script seeks the `<video>` mid-frame, fighting the FFI capture FFmpeg pipeline). Test (x) source-grep enforces both values + the NDI exemption.

**PROCESS GR — When the same operator-visible bug ("output stutter / micro-freezes") has both a SENDER cause (high broadcast frequency) AND a RECEIVER cause (over-aggressive sync correction), fix BOTH in the same release**: fixing only the sender (raise writeback threshold) reduces broadcast rate but leaves the receiver's `0.20s` tolerance triggering on any remaining jitter spike → still stutters, just less often. Fixing only the receiver (raise tolerance) suppresses seeks but keeps the CPU storm running (operator's machine still does ~10Hz JSON.stringify of ~80-field payload). Both must be fixed together: raise sender threshold to cap broadcast rate, AND raise receiver tolerance to ignore the remaining jitter floor. The intermediate window (writeback every 0.50s with receiver tolerance 0.20s) would actually be WORSE than v0.7.216 because the receiver would seek on every writeback (each one is a 0.50s jump, far above 0.20s).

**PROCESS GR — When a payload field is broadcast at high frequency, the cost is multiplied by EVERY consumer**: pre-v0.7.217, `mediaCurrentTime` writeback at 10Hz × ~80-field payload × ~3-4KB JSON × N connected receivers (1-3 secondary screens + 1 NDI capture window + 1+ external NDI viewers) = ~30-50KB/sec network out per operator. v0.7.217 drops this 5x to ~6-10KB/sec. Future audit: any new field added to the output payload should be reviewed for write frequency × receiver count; high-frequency fields (anything that ticks during playback) should consult `writeThreshold` or an equivalent throttle, not write unconditionally on every change.

---

## v0.7.216 — Single-clicking a DIFFERENT media tile while LIVE plays a media-video MUST NOT stall the live video (operator $1600-customer escalation, follow-up to v0.7.210 / v0.7.212)

Operator on v0.7.215: "At Media column when users single click on another media, the one that's playing on the live display stops playing." Per the user's standing requirement (v0.7.210): single-click in Media is **preview-only** and MUST NEVER interfere with live playback. v0.7.210 fixed the store-mutation path (`sendMediaToPreview` swapped legacy `setSlides + setLiveSlideIndex(-1)` for `pinPreviewSlide` so `liveSlide` / `slides[]` / `isLive` stay untouched). v0.7.212 fixed the iframe transport path (`output-preview.tsx` `sendNow` adds a cheap-fingerprint dedup so byte-identical mirrorLive payloads don't tear down the iframe `<video>`). v0.7.216 fixes the **third** path — React-side hardware decoder competition for the in-app Live Display.

**Root cause / pre-state**: For media-video on Live, `logos-shell.tsx` LiveDisplayCard (L1841-1857) does **NOT** route through the mirrorLive iframe — it mounts `<MediaVideoSurface surface="live" src={liveSlide.mediaUrl}>` directly so the operator's transport bar can scrub/pause the local HTMLMediaElement. PreviewCard (L1116-1170) uses the same `MediaVideoSurface` for `previewIsVideoMedia` slides, with `autoPlay={surface === 'preview'}` at L421. When LIVE was already playing video A and the operator single-clicked a DIFFERENT video B in the Media column, `sendMediaToPreview(B)` → `pinPreviewSlide(B)` correctly mutated only `pinnedPreviewSlide`. But the PreviewCard re-render then mounted a SECOND `<video>` element for B with `autoPlay=true` → adding a 2nd hardware video decoder slot. On the GPUs operators run (cap 2-4 concurrent HW decoders), the new decoder competes with the live decoder and the live `<video>` stalls/pauses mid-playback. v0.7.193-hotfix.2 handled only the SAME-media case (PreviewCard short-circuits to an "ON AIR" placard at L1132-1147, no 2nd `<video>` mounts). The DIFFERENT-media case was never addressed. Symptoms surfaced now because operator workflows increasingly chain media clips — staging the next clip while the current one plays.

**Fix #1 (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L421-434, MediaVideoSurface `<video>` autoPlay attr)**: gate the preview-only autoplay on `!mediaPaused`. Change `autoPlay={surface === 'preview'}` → `autoPlay={surface === 'preview' && !mediaPaused}`. The `mediaPaused` prop is already wired from the store (`previewMediaPaused` for preview surface, `mediaPaused` field on the slide for live). When `mediaPaused === true` at mount, the HTML `autoplay` attribute is absent → the browser never calls `play()` on the element → no HW decoder slot is requested → no competition with the live decoder. Operator can still start playback explicitly via the VideoTransport bar's Play button (sets `previewMediaPaused=false`, the existing play/pause `useEffect` at L379-381 then calls `v.play()` — the operator's explicit intent).

**Fix #2 (`src/components/layout/library-compact.tsx` L1290-1323, `sendMediaToPreview`)**: detect when LIVE is currently playing a DIFFERENT media-video. Read `liveSlide` via the v0.7.208 direct-ref-first pattern (`st.liveSlide ?? (st.liveSlideIndex >= 0 ? st.slides[st.liveSlideIndex] : null)`); evaluate `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo = liveSlide.type==='media' && liveSlide.mediaKind==='video' && liveSlide.mediaUrl && m.kind==='video' && slide.mediaUrl && liveSlide.mediaUrl !== slide.mediaUrl`. When true, **before** calling `pinPreviewSlide(slide)`, flip `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)` and `setPreviewMediaCurrentTime(0)`. This guarantees the new `MediaVideoSurface` mounts with `mediaPaused=true` from the very first render → Fix #1's gated `autoPlay` returns `false` → no autoplay → no 2nd decoder. Three properties hold simultaneously: (a) preview IS pinned (operator sees the new clip's first frame and can scrub via the transport bar); (b) live state is COMPLETELY untouched (`liveSlide`, `liveSlideIndex`, `slides[]`, `isLive` all preserved — `buildOutputPayload` returns the identical payload, broadcaster's JSON.stringify dedup short-circuits any SSE re-post, secondary screen / NDI projector continue playing video A uninterrupted); (c) the GPU's HW decoder count stays at 1 instead of momentarily climbing to 2. Same-media case is unchanged — it short-circuits to the v0.7.193-hotfix.2 "ON AIR" placard branch in PreviewCard, no 2nd `<video>` ever mounts there either.

**No change needed at the broadcaster / SSE / iframe layer**: `buildOutputPayload` (`src/lib/output-payload.ts`) never reads `pinnedPreviewSlide` — it returns `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]`. Since v0.7.216 leaves all of `liveSlide`, `liveSlideIndex`, and `slides[]` untouched on a different-media preview click, the payload returned to the broadcaster is byte-identical. The OutputBroadcaster's `JSON.stringify(payload) === lastSentJson` early-return at L262-265 short-circuits → no `/api/output` POST → no SSE event → secondary screen / NDI projector receive nothing → their `<video>` elements continue playback uninterrupted. For the in-app Live Display mirrorLive iframe path (used for NON-media-video live slides where LiveDisplayCard falls back to `<OutputPreview mirrorLive>`), v0.7.212's `sendNow` fingerprint dedup at `output-preview.tsx` L361-393 already prevents the iframe `<video>` from being torn down.

**New regression tests (4, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (q) BEHAVIOURAL — stage LIVE playing video A via legacy GO LIVE path (`slides=[liveVid]`, `liveSlideIndex=0`, `isLive=true`, `previewMediaPaused=false`, `previewMediaCurrentTime=42.5`); simulate the EXACT v0.7.216 sendMediaToPreview mutation sequence for a DIFFERENT video B (`setPreviewMediaPaused(true) + setPreviewMediaCurrentTime(0) + pinPreviewSlide(B)`); assert `pinnedPreviewSlide.id === 'vid-new'` AND `previewMediaPaused === true` AND `previewMediaCurrentTime === 0` (preview correctly pinned + paused so no autoplay/decoder); assert `slides[0].id === 'vid-live'`, `liveSlideIndex === 0`, `liveSlide === null`, `isLive === true` (live untouched); assert `buildOutputPayload(state).slide.id === 'vid-live'` (broadcaster payload identical, secondary screen unaffected). (r) BEHAVIOURAL — same-media case: stage LIVE playing video A, simulate sendMediaToPreview with same-mediaUrl preview clone; assert `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo === false`, the pause-before-pin branch is SKIPPED, `previewMediaPaused` stays `false` (operator's transport state preserved, v0.7.193-hotfix.2 PreviewCard placard branch handles the rest). (s) GUARD source-grep — `logos-shell.tsx` MediaVideoSurface `<video>` `autoPlay` attribute MUST match `!mediaPaused` AND `surface === 'preview'`. (t) GUARD source-grep — `library-compact.tsx` `sendMediaToPreview` body MUST contain `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo` detection, `mediaUrl !== slide.mediaUrl` comparison, `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)`, `setPreviewMediaCurrentTime(0)`, AND still call `pinPreviewSlide(slide)`; MUST NOT call `setSlides(` / `setLiveSlideIndex(` / `setIsLive(` / `setLiveAuto(` (re-asserts v0.7.210 independence invariants).

**Also widened (`src/lib/theme-bg-visibility.test.ts` L116-127 J GUARD)**: regex `/const sendMediaToPreview[^}]+pinPreviewSlide\(slide\)/s` widened to `/const sendMediaToPreview[\s\S]*?pinPreviewSlide\(slide\)/` because the v0.7.216 sendMediaToPreview now contains an `if {...}` block (pause-before-pin) BEFORE the pin call, so the `[^}]+` "no closing braces" constraint no longer holds. The primitive call itself is still asserted. Same widening applied symmetrically to the `sendMediaToLive` / `setLiveAuto` companion guard for consistency.

**Test results**: 20/20 PASS in `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` (was 16/16 on v0.7.215 — +4). Full suite **654/654 PASS** (was 650/650 on v0.7.215 — +4). 0 regressions.

**Replit runtime proof**: test (q) exercises the EXACT 3-call store mutation sequence the production sendMediaToPreview performs (`setPreviewMediaPaused(true) + setPreviewMediaCurrentTime(0) + pinPreviewSlide(B)`); the assertion `buildOutputPayload.slide.id === 'vid-live'` mirrors the broadcaster's read path through the v0.7.203 output-payload contract — proves that the broadcaster sees an identical payload and therefore the SSE/iframe stack stays silent. Source-grep guards (s)+(t) prove the fixes live at the call sites (logos-shell.tsx L421-434 + library-compact.tsx L1290-1323), not in a wrapper that could be bypassed. Same hybrid proof shape as v0.7.210/v0.7.212/v0.7.213/v0.7.214/v0.7.215 (architect-accepted).

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — `MediaVideoSurface` `<video>` `autoPlay` attribute MUST be gated on `!mediaPaused` in addition to `surface === 'preview'`**: removing the `!mediaPaused` clause re-introduces the unconditional preview autoplay → 2nd HW decoder spins up the instant a new preview clip mounts → competes with live decoder → live `<video>` stalls. The browser's `autoplay` attribute is processed at element load (synchronously with mount); React's `useEffect` play/pause logic runs AFTER. Without the attribute gate, the `useEffect` would race the browser's autoplay attempt — the decoder may still be allocated before the effect's `v.pause()` lands. Test (s) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — `sendMediaToPreview` MUST flip `previewMediaPaused=true` + reset clock to 0 BEFORE `pinPreviewSlide(slide)` when `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo === true`**: ordering matters. If `pinPreviewSlide` runs first, React's render scheduler may commit a frame where `pinnedPreviewSlide` is the new clip but `previewMediaPaused` is still the old value → `MediaVideoSurface` mounts with `autoPlay=true` for one frame → decoder is requested. The pause-then-pin order ensures that by the time the new `MediaVideoSurface` mounts and reads `mediaPaused` from the store, the flag is already `true`. Test (q) verifies the post-state but the ordering is enforced by the source code — future refactors must preserve the order.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — `sendMediaToPreview` MUST NOT touch `slides[]` / `liveSlideIndex` / `isLive` / `setLiveAuto` under ANY condition (independence from v0.7.210)**: the v0.7.216 detection branch ONLY mutates preview-scoped store fields (`previewMediaPaused`, `previewMediaCurrentTime`, `pinnedPreviewSlide`). Any future addition that mutates live state under the "pause-before-pin" pretext re-opens the v0.7.209-era bug class. Test (t) `not.toMatch(...)` enforces ABSENCE of all 4 live-side primitives.

**GUARD-RAIL (D) — Same-media case (`mediaUrl === liveSlide.mediaUrl`) MUST NOT enter the pause-before-pin branch**: same-media short-circuits at the PreviewCard "ON AIR" placard (v0.7.193-hotfix.2) — no 2nd `<video>` mounts there, so no decoder competition exists to prevent. Force-pausing the operator's existing preview transport state in that case would be a surprise regression (operator could be mid-scrub on the same clip that's also on Live for an A/B preview workflow). Test (r) explicitly verifies `previewMediaPaused` stays `false` after a same-media re-pin.

**GUARD-RAIL (E) — `liveSlide` anchor read inside `sendMediaToPreview` MUST use the v0.7.208 direct-ref-first pattern (`st.liveSlide ?? (st.liveSlideIndex >= 0 ? st.slides[st.liveSlideIndex] : null)`)**: bare `slides[liveSlideIndex]` would return `null` when an AI-detected video is live (AI path leaves `liveSlideIndex=-1` and writes directly to `liveSlide` per v0.7.203/v0.7.208), causing `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo` to evaluate `false` — the pause-before-pin branch would skip and the decoder competition bug would re-surface for AI-driven live scenarios. Source code at library-compact.tsx L1308 documents this; future audit: any new reader of "the currently-live slide" MUST use this same pattern.

**PROCESS GR — When the same operator-visible bug ("clicking media B stops live video A") has multiple INDEPENDENT failure paths (store mutation in v0.7.210, iframe transport dedup in v0.7.212, React decoder competition in v0.7.216), the fix sequence is CORRECT — not duplicative**: each release closed one layer of the bug. A "comprehensive single-shot fix" would have required understanding all 3 paths simultaneously, which was not knowable until each layer surfaced under operator load. The forward audit pattern when this bug class re-surfaces: enumerate every consumer of the shadowed state (`pinnedPreviewSlide`, `mediaUrl`, `isLive` cross-pane reads) and classify each as (1) store mutation, (2) network/SSE transport, (3) DOM/React rendering, (4) browser-resource (decoder, AudioContext) competition. Each class needs an independent test guard.

**PROCESS GR — When an HTML media attribute (`autoplay`, `muted`, `loop`, `preload`) controls a finite hardware resource, gating it conditionally is preferable to gating only the JS-side `play()` call**: the browser's attribute processing runs at element creation, BEFORE React's `useEffect` mount commits. Even a perfectly-correct `useEffect` that calls `v.pause()` immediately may race the browser's attribute-driven allocation. Always gate at the attribute layer when the attribute exists; reserve `useEffect` for stateful transitions (play → pause → play) after the element is mounted.

### Follow-up #1 — GO LIVE swap of a paused-by-v0.7.216 preview clip MUST auto-play the moment the new live src buffers

**Operator escalation (same release window)**: "User clicking on Go Live also send video from preview to live display to immediate start playing." Screenshot: Preview shows a paused media-video (person at mic, transport at 19:14), LIVE shows a different media-video (storm), arrow on the rose GO LIVE button. Operator expectation: pressing GO LIVE promotes the preview clip to live AND it starts playing immediately.

**Root cause**: The interaction with the main v0.7.216 fix exposed a load race in `MediaVideoSurface`. Pre-state of LIVE is already a media-video → `<MediaVideoSurface surface="live" src={A.mediaUrl}>` is mounted. Operator clicks a different media tile B → v0.7.216 main fix flips `previewMediaPaused=true` + `previewMediaCurrentTime=0` + `pinPreviewSlide(B)` (preview mounts paused at t=0, no 2nd decoder, live keeps playing A). Operator presses GO LIVE → v0.7.212 `goLive` pinned-path runs: capture `pv.currentTime` (= 0, because v0.7.216 reset it), `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(0) + setLiveMediaPaused(false) + setPreviewMediaPaused(true) + setLiveAuto(B)` (sets `liveSlide=B + isLive=true`). React **REUSES** the live `<MediaVideoSurface>` element at the same position with a new `src` attribute (same component type, same position in tree). Browser semantics: changing `src` aborts the current playback, fires `emptied`, and starts loading the new resource. The play/pause `useEffect` at L370 fires synchronously in the same render commit — BEFORE the new src has buffered (readyState is back to 0). `shouldPlay = isLive && !mediaPaused = true` → `v.play().catch(() => {})` is called immediately, but on most browsers (including Electron's Chromium) `play()` on a freshly-swapped src that hasn't reached `HAVE_FUTURE_DATA` either drops silently or queues until `canplay` and then can be lost if any other state mutation re-renders the surface in between. Net behaviour: live pane stalls on the first frame of B until the next user action.

**Fix (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L382-409, +22 lines in the `MediaVideoSurface` play/pause effect)**: keep the synchronous `v.play()` (handles the steady-state case where src is already buffered), and ADD a one-shot `canplay` listener that retries `v.play()` the moment the new src reaches `HAVE_FUTURE_DATA`. The listener is registered with `{ once: true }` so it self-removes on first fire; the effect cleanup also explicitly removes it so a rapid second src-swap (operator promotes B then immediately swaps to C) doesn't leak listeners or fire stale retries on the previous src. No change to the deps array — `[surface, isLive, mediaPaused, mediaCurrentTime, src]` already correctly re-runs the effect on every src swap.

**Why this is the right layer**: the goLive callback (logos-shell.tsx L4022-4046) cannot reach into the `<video>` element directly (videoRef is private to MediaVideoSurface), and a synchronous `document.querySelector('video[data-surface="live"]')` immediately after `setLiveAuto` would return the OLD element (React hasn't re-rendered yet — `setLiveAuto` is just a state schedule). Adding a store-level "force play sentinel" would require N matching effects across surfaces. The `canplay` retry inside MediaVideoSurface is the minimal local fix: it handles every src-swap path (GO LIVE pinned promotion, AI auto-detect re-routing live to a media-video, schedule advance, voice-command jump-to-N) uniformly without leaking implementation details into call sites.

**No change needed at**: the goLive callback (already correctly orders `setLiveMediaPaused(false)` BEFORE `setLiveAuto`), the broadcaster (live media-video doesn't route through SSE for the in-app Live Display — the iframe-mirrorLive consumers have their own `<video>` elements that face the SAME load race; the `canplay` retry in `output-preview.tsx`'s media path would be a separate audit), or the store (no new fields).

**New regression test (1, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (u) GUARD — anchor on the play/pause effect's `shouldPlay = surface === 'live' ? (isLive && !mediaPaused) : !mediaPaused` opener through to the closing deps array `[surface, isLive, mediaPaused, mediaCurrentTime, src]`. Assert: (1) synchronous `v.play().catch(() => {})` is still present (steady-state path unbroken); (2) one-shot `canplay` listener is registered with `{ once: true }` (no manual cleanup-of-cleanup needed if it fires); (3) `onCanPlay` body calls `v.play().catch(() => {})` (retry is real, not a stub); (4) cleanup MUST `removeEventListener('canplay', onCanPlay)` (rapid src-swap doesn't leak).

**Test results**: 21/21 PASS in `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` (was 20/20 after main v0.7.216 — +1). Full suite **655/655 PASS** (was 654/654 — +1). 0 regressions.

**GUARD-RAIL (F) — `MediaVideoSurface` play/pause effect MUST register a one-shot `canplay` retry inside the `shouldPlay` branch, with cleanup**: removing the retry re-introduces the load-race stall on every src-swap path. The synchronous `v.play()` alone is insufficient because `play()` on a freshly-swapped src in `readyState=0` is browser-implementation-defined — Chromium/Electron drop it silently in the Replit reference build. Test (u) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (G) — `canplay` listener MUST be `{ once: true }` AND removed in cleanup**: `once:true` alone is insufficient because if the effect re-runs (e.g. operator rapidly swaps clips B → C → D) before the previous `canplay` fires, the previous listener is still attached to the same `<video>` element (it's the same DOM node, only `src` changed) — when `canplay` finally fires for C's src, the listener may attempt to `play()` after D's src has already taken over, calling `play()` on the wrong intended state. Explicit `removeEventListener` in the effect's cleanup function severs the stale listener on every effect re-run. Both halves of the guard are required.

**PROCESS GR — When a previous fix in the SAME release window reshapes the pre-state of a downstream behaviour (here, v0.7.216 main fix made preview start paused at t=0 before GO LIVE was likely pressed), audit every downstream consumer for assumptions about the OLD pre-state**: pre-v0.7.216, preview was always already playing when operator pressed GO LIVE → preview's `<video>` was at `readyState=HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA` → if it was swapped to live, the live element inherited a buffered src → `play()` worked first try. v0.7.216 paused preview at t=0 → preview's `<video>` may still be loading when GO LIVE fires → live's src-swap starts from `readyState=0` → load race surfaces. The follow-up fix lives at the right architectural layer (MediaVideoSurface, where the race actually occurs) rather than reverting v0.7.216's pause-before-pin behaviour. Forward audit pattern: every release that changes a pre-state of UI gesture A → audit every UI gesture B that consumes A's post-state for implicit "A was already in steady-state" assumptions.

### Follow-up #2 — Settings dropdowns MUST render ABOVE the Settings overlay (and remain visible while a media-video is playing on Live)

**Operator escalation (same release window)**: "When media video is playing, and users move to Setting and click any of the setting dropdown, all the dropdown doesn't show." Screenshot: Settings page open with red arrows pointing at FONT, SIZE, DROP SHADOW, ALIGN, CAPTURE RESOLUTION, CAPTURE FRAME RATE, BAR HEIGHT dropdowns — none of them showed their options panel when clicked.

**Root cause**: The Settings overlay in `src/app/page.tsx` L76 is `<div className="fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-background flex flex-col">` — full-screen z-50 with an opaque background. Every Radix portal-based UI primitive (Select, DropdownMenu, Popover, Tooltip, HoverCard, ContextMenu, Menubar, NavigationMenu, Dialog, AlertDialog, Sheet) was ALSO set to `z-50` in `src/components/ui/*.tsx`. When two sibling portals under `<body>` have the same z-index, CSS stacking falls back to DOM order — the LATER-mounted element wins. The settings overlay is `aria-hidden`-ing the LogosShell wrapper but NOT unmounting it, so the live `MediaVideoSurface` `<video>` keeps decoding/painting in a hardware-composited layer. Every video frame triggers a compositor repaint on the live console wrapper, and on Chromium/Electron this can re-stack same-z sibling portals — the dropdown gets re-painted BEHIND the settings overlay's opaque `bg-background`, so the operator clicks the trigger, the portal mounts, but its options panel is invisible.

**Fix (`src/components/ui/{select,dropdown-menu,popover,tooltip,hover-card,context-menu,menubar,navigation-menu,dialog,alert-dialog,sheet}.tsx`, 19 z-class swaps across 11 files)**: bump every `z-50` on a portal Content / Viewport / Overlay class to `z-[60]`. Settings overlay stays at `z-50` (load-bearing baseline — it must beat the LogosShell which is z-0/auto, but the portals must beat IT). 11 files × 1-2 z-classes each = 19 total swaps. The bump is uniform across all portal-based primitives because any of them used inside Settings (or any other future fullscreen overlay) would have the same bug — fixing only `select.tsx` would leave `Popover`/`Tooltip`/etc. as silent landmines.

**No change needed at**: `src/app/page.tsx` (settings overlay z-50 stays — it's the documented baseline this guard depends on), the live MediaVideoSurface (the compositor repaint is correct behaviour for a playing video; the bug was that we let portals tie with the overlay), the store / SSE / broadcaster.

**New regression test (1, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (v) GUARD — read `src/app/page.tsx` and assert the settings overlay STILL uses `fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-background` (load-bearing baseline). Then for every popover-portal primitive file in the list above, assert the source MUST NOT contain bare `z-50` AND MUST contain `z-[60]`. If a future shadcn/ui re-sync pulls in fresh `z-50` defaults, this guard fails immediately rather than silently regressing operator-visible dropdown behaviour.

**Runtime proof (Playwright via testing skill)**: Test navigated to `/`, opened Settings via sidebar nav → "Settings", scrolled to NDI Typography section, clicked the FONT Radix Select trigger, verified the Select content portal exists in the DOM, opacity = 1, fully visible in viewport, with 13 selectable items rendered inside. PASSED with status `success` — the dropdown opens above the settings overlay as intended.

**Test results**: 22/22 PASS in `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` (was 21/21 — +1). Full suite **656/656 PASS** (was 655/655 — +1). 0 regressions. (One transient test (v) failure during authoring was a regex `\b` boundary bug — `\bz-\[60\]\b` doesn't match because `\b` doesn't fire next to `[`/`]`; fixed by dropping the boundaries: `/z-\[60\]/`. The fix's actual source changes were already in place when the test failed — the regex was the bug, not the fix.)

**GUARD-RAIL (H) — EVERY Radix portal-based UI primitive (Select, DropdownMenu, Popover, Tooltip, HoverCard, ContextMenu, Menubar, NavigationMenu, Dialog, AlertDialog, Sheet) MUST use `z-[60]`, NEVER bare `z-50`**: bare `z-50` ties with the settings overlay and can render BEHIND it on any frame where a HW-composited layer (live video, NDI preview iframe) re-stacks the portal. Test (v) source-grep enforces across all 11 primitive files. Future shadcn/ui re-syncs must be audited — the upstream default is `z-50`; we deliberately diverge.

**GUARD-RAIL (I) — Settings overlay MUST stay at `z-50 fixed inset-0 bg-background`**: this is the documented baseline guard (v) depends on. Moving it to `z-[60]` or higher would mean every portal needs to be bumped again. Moving it to a non-fixed positioning would let the LogosShell paint over it on certain scroll states. Test (v) source-grep enforces the exact class string.

**PROCESS GR — When two sibling stacking contexts must have a deterministic z-order, NEVER let them tie**: equal-z siblings fall back to DOM order, which is non-deterministic for portals (mount order depends on render commit order, which depends on React scheduler). Always give the higher layer a STRICTLY greater z-index (delta ≥ 10 to leave room for intermediates). When introducing a new fullscreen overlay layer, audit every existing portal-based primitive for its z-index and pick the overlay's z-index to be STRICTLY less than all portal z-indexes — OR bump all portals in one shot (as v0.7.216 follow-up #2 did). The bump-portals approach is preferable when the overlay's z-index is load-bearing for OTHER layout decisions (here, beating the LogosShell wrapper).

**PROCESS GR — When a UI bug is reported with the framing "X breaks when Y is happening" (here, "dropdowns don't show when media video is playing"), Y is often a SYMPTOM AMPLIFIER, not the cause**: the actual bug (same-z tie between settings overlay and dropdown portals) existed independent of any video playing. A playing video amplified it because every video frame triggered a compositor repaint that could re-stack the portals; without a playing video, the bug would surface intermittently on other repaint triggers (window resize, animation frame, etc.) — possibly so rarely that operators never reported it. Forward audit pattern: when a bug report includes a specific "when X" trigger, file the trigger as a symptom amplifier and look for an underlying structural bug that exists without X.

## v0.7.215 — Per-pane Clear buttons on Preview and Live Display (independent, single-click; operator UX request)

Operator on v0.7.214: "Add a clear button on both preview and live display that users can clear anything on there with a single click. Both shouldn't interfere with each other." Screenshot circled the empty footer strip at the bottom of each pane in `logos-shell.tsx` as the placement target.

**Root cause / pre-state**: `logos-shell.tsx` had a `clearLive` callback at L4044 (only called from the GO LIVE / STOP LIVE toggle button when `isLive === true`, and from the keyboard Escape / Enter-while-live shortcut), but no UI affordance to wipe Live when the toggle was idle (e.g. an AI-driven slide held purely in the v0.7.203 `liveSlide` direct ref with `isLive=false`). There was also no Clear primitive for the Preview pane — `clearPinnedPreview()` existed in the store (L1191) but no button surfaced it, so once a verse pinned via v0.7.201 `pinPreviewSlide` it sat there until a new pin or a `setSlides([…])` reset replaced it. Operator routinely landed in states where one pane was visibly populated but no single-click affordance to clear it existed.

Additionally, the existing `clearLive` callback was incomplete: it called `setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false) + sendToOutput(null,false)` but did NOT call `clearLiveAuto()`. That meant a stale v0.7.203 `liveSlide` direct ref survived a Clear (and Stop Live, and Escape, and Enter-while-live) — the next broadcaster tick reading `buildOutputPayload`'s `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` would re-render the AI-detected verse even though the operator thought it was cleared. Latent since v0.7.203 (Jul 2025), surfaced now because we're putting a "Clear" label on the affordance and the operator's mental contract demands the pane actually go empty.

**Fix #1 (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L4044-4058, +6 lines)**: prepend `useAppStore.getState().clearLiveAuto()` to `clearLive`. Now `clearLive = clearLiveAuto() + setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false) + sendToOutput(null,false)` — wipes the direct ref alongside the legacy state so Live truly goes empty. Inline v0.7.215 comment documents the latent-since-v0.7.203 gap and why the new Clear button forced us to close it.

**Fix #2 (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L1180-1205, ~25 lines net)**: the PreviewCard symmetry strip (previously an empty `<div className="h-7" />` spacer that existed purely to mirror LiveDisplayCard's bottom transport row height) now hosts an explicit Clear Preview `<Button>` with the `CircleSlash` icon. `onClick` calls the EXACT 2 primitives the Preview pane needs: `useAppStore.getState().clearPinnedPreview()` (wipes v0.7.201 pin) + `useAppStore.getState().setPreviewSlideIndex(-1)` (resets index so the `pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex]` read returns `null`). MUST NOT touch `slides[]` (operator's deck survives — Clear is not "remove all"), `liveSlide` / `liveSlideIndex` (live keeps playing), or `isLive` (broadcast keeps going). Button is disabled when `!previewSlide` so the operator can see at a glance whether there's anything to clear. PreviewCard already subscribes to the store, so no prop drilling needed.

**Fix #3 (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L1942-1968, ~18 lines)**: the LiveDisplayCard bottom toolbar (Prev | Go Live/Stop Live | Next) gains a 4th button — Clear Live — inserted to the LEFT of Prev. `onClick={onClearLive}` reuses the existing prop (which the parent shell wires to the v0.7.215-updated `clearLive` callback at L4044). Always visible (unlike the Stop Live button which only appears when `isLive === true`), with `disabled={!liveSlide && !isLive}` so the operator can wipe a stale direct-ref slide even when the GO LIVE toggle is idle. Uses the same `CircleSlash` icon as Clear Preview for visual symmetry. Inline v0.7.215 comment documents that this is intentionally a SEPARATE button from Stop Live (Stop Live is conditional and overloaded with the GO LIVE toggle; Clear Live is unconditional and single-purpose).

**No change needed in `easyworship-shell.tsx`**: the operator screenshot is clearly the logos shell (left-hand pane chrome, header layout, audio meter rails, schedule feed card). EasyWorship shell uses a different layout chassis with the existing Stop Live + Clear toggle at the bottom toolbar already covering the same UX. If the operator escalates the same UX gap there in a future release, the same 3-fix pattern applies (audit clearLive completeness + add per-pane Clear buttons with independence guards).

**New regression tests (5, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**:
- **(l) BEHAVIOURAL — Clear Preview wipes preview but live keeps playing**: operator stages a verse on Preview via `pinPreviewSlide(previewVerse)` AND a verse on Live via `setLiveAuto(liveVerse)` + `setIsLive(true)`. Mirrors the new button's EXACT primitives: `clearPinnedPreview() + setPreviewSlideIndex(-1)`. Asserts (i) preview reads `pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex] === null`, (ii) `liveSlide.id === 'rom-8-28'` (untouched), (iii) `liveSlideIndex === 0` (untouched), (iv) `isLive === true` (untouched), (v) `buildOutputPayload(state).slide.id === 'rom-8-28'` (output still renders live), (vi) `slides[0].id === 'rom-8-28'` (deck survives).
- **(m) BEHAVIOURAL — Clear Live wipes live but preview keeps its pin**: symmetric setup. Mirrors the new clearLive's EXACT primitives: `clearLiveAuto() + setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false)`. Asserts (i) `liveSlide === null` (direct ref wiped — v0.7.215's Fix #1), (ii) `liveSlideIndex === -1`, (iii) `isLive === false`, (iv) `pinnedPreviewSlide?.id === 'john-3-16'` (untouched), (v) preview read still yields the pinned verse, (vi) `slides[0].id === 'rom-8-28'` (deck survives).
- **(n) GUARD — `clearLive` source MUST call `clearLiveAuto()` so the v0.7.203 direct ref is wiped**: regex anchors on the `const clearLive = useCallback(() => {` block; asserts `clearLiveAuto()`, `setLiveSlideIndex(-1)`, `setIsLive(false)`, `sendToOutput(null, false)` all present. Reverting Fix #1 (removing `clearLiveAuto()`) fails this test.
- **(o) GUARD — PreviewCard symmetry strip MUST host a Clear Preview button wired to `clearPinnedPreview()` + `setPreviewSlideIndex(-1)` ONLY**: regex anchors on the v0.7.215 marker comment through `Clear Preview </Button>`. Asserts presence of `clearPinnedPreview()` AND `setPreviewSlideIndex(-1)`. Asserts ABSENCE of `setIsLive(`, `setLiveSlideIndex(`, `clearLiveAuto(`, `setSlides(` — the independence invariant. Reverting Fix #2 or sneaking a live-side mutation into the button fails this test.
- **(p) GUARD — LiveDisplayCard bottom toolbar MUST host a Clear Live button wired to `onClearLive` (independent of GO LIVE toggle)**: regex anchors on the v0.7.215 marker through `Clear </Button>`. Asserts `onClick={onClearLive}`, ABSENCE of `onSendLive` (proves it's separate from the GO LIVE / STOP LIVE toggle), ABSENCE of `clearPinnedPreview` and `setPreviewSlideIndex` inline (proves no preview-side mutation slipped in). Reverting Fix #3 fails this test.

**Test results**: 16/16 PASS in `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` (was 11/11 on v0.7.213 — +5). Full suite **650/650 PASS** (was 645/645 on v0.7.214 — +5). 0 regressions.

**Replit runtime proof**: not separately scripted — tests (l) and (m) exercise the EXACT store mutation sequences the new buttons trigger (verified by source-grep guards (o) and (p) that those primitives ARE what's wired to the buttons' onClick handlers); the `buildOutputPayload` assertion in (l) mirrors the broadcaster's read path through the v0.7.203 output-payload contract. Source-grep guards (n)+(o)+(p) prove the fixes live at the call sites (not in a wrapper that could be bypassed). Same hybrid proof shape as v0.7.208 / v0.7.210 / v0.7.212 / v0.7.213 / v0.7.214 (architect-accepted).

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — `logos-shell.tsx` `clearLive` callback MUST call `clearLiveAuto()` BEFORE the legacy `setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false) + sendToOutput(null,false)` sequence**: omitting `clearLiveAuto()` leaves the v0.7.203 `liveSlide` direct ref alive, and `buildOutputPayload`'s `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` read will re-render the stale AI-detected verse on the next broadcaster tick — the operator presses Clear / Stop Live / Escape / Enter-while-live and the pane *looks* cleared in the local React tree but the projector / NDI feed keeps showing the AI slide. Test (n) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — Clear Preview button MUST call ONLY `clearPinnedPreview() + setPreviewSlideIndex(-1)`, NOTHING ELSE**: any extra mutation (`setSlides([])`, `setIsLive(false)`, `setLiveSlideIndex(-1)`, `clearLiveAuto()`) breaks the independence contract the operator escalated for. `setSlides([])` would destroy the operator's deck (Clear is not "remove all" — that's what the Scripture Feed's removeAllScheduleItems / removeAllVerseHistory bulk-delete affordances are for). Touching any live-side primitive would make clicking Clear Preview cascade into live — the exact "interference" the operator's request explicitly forbids. Test (o) `.not.toMatch(...)` enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — Clear Live button MUST call ONLY `onClearLive` (the prop), which in turn calls ONLY the v0.7.215 4-primitive sequence**: the button's `onClick` MUST NOT inline any preview-side mutation. The 4-primitive sequence MUST stay symmetric: `clearLiveAuto()` (direct ref) → `setLiveSlideIndex(-1)` (legacy index) → `setIsLive(false)` (broadcast flag) → `sendToOutput(null, false)` (projector / NDI push). Removing any of the 4 leaves a partial-clear that produces operator-visible drift between the React tree, the store, and the projector. Tests (n)+(p) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (D) — Clear buttons MUST be visually distinct from Stop Live**: the Clear Live button uses `CircleSlash` + "CLEAR" label + neutral border styling; the Stop Live button uses `Square` + "STOP LIVE" label + rose-bordered muted styling (the conditional inverse of the rose-filled GO LIVE button). The two are placed adjacent in the toolbar so operators don't conflate them. Conflating Clear (always available, wipes the pane) with Stop Live (conditional on isLive, kills the broadcast) re-introduces the v0.7.214-era confusion class where operator pressed GO LIVE → toggle became Stop Live → operator pressed it again expecting "clear" but only stopped the broadcast while a stale slide stayed visible.

**PROCESS GR — When the operator-visible label of an affordance is "Clear", the underlying primitive MUST wipe EVERY layer of the state model that contributes to the rendered output**: for Live, that's the direct ref (v0.7.203 `liveSlide`) + the legacy index (`liveSlideIndex`) + the broadcast flag (`isLive`) + the projector push (`sendToOutput`). For Preview, that's the direct ref (v0.7.201 `pinnedPreviewSlide`) + the legacy index (`previewSlideIndex`). Any layer omitted is a latent regression waiting for an operator to hit the state combination where THAT layer is what's driving the pane. The right release-time question for a "Clear X" affordance: `rg -n "<X>Slide|<X>SlideIndex|<X>:\s*null|set<X>" src/lib/store.ts` THEN enumerate every state field that contributes to `buildOutputPayload`'s read path for surface X, and verify the Clear primitive wipes ALL of them.

**PROCESS GR — A "symmetry strip" empty spacer in a card layout is technical debt that signals a missing affordance**: PreviewCard's L1180-1197 `<div className="h-7" />` placeholder existed since v0.7.157 purely to keep the 16:9 stage size matched between Preview and Live (otherwise Preview's body had more vertical real estate and the two stages rendered at different sizes side-by-side). Operator-visible UX never registered this; the placeholder was load-bearing for layout but content-less. v0.7.215's Clear Preview button fills it with the EXACT affordance the layout was reserving space for. Future audit: when a card layout has a `<div className="h-N" />` aria-hidden placeholder, treat it as a backlog candidate — the height reservation is correct but the content is missing.

## v0.7.214 — Voice commands & AI/LLM verse navigation MUST target LIVE display only ($1600-customer escalation, follow-up to v0.7.208/v0.7.213)

Operator on v0.7.213: "I want voice commands to apply to what is LIVE display, not preview, because AI detection now auto-sends to live. User said 'KJV version' and it only applied to preview, leaving the AI-detected verse on live unchanged. Also when user says 'next verse' / 'next chapter' / jump-to-number, it goes for the PREVIEW bible text instead of the AI-detected LIVE one. Disconnect preview from anything AI does."

**Root cause (`src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx`, 8 sites)**: v0.7.208 fixed the "KJV switches preview not live" symptom at the `live-translation-sync.tsx` layer (translation watcher now consults `s.liveSlide` before `slides[liveSlideIndex]`). But the voice-command handlers in `speech-provider.tsx` were never audited for the same pattern. When AI auto-detect fires through the v0.7.203 `setLiveAuto(slide)` primitive, it writes to the `liveSlide` DIRECT REF and leaves `liveSlideIndex = -1` (or some stale operator value). The output payload reads `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` — the AI ref wins on screen. But every voice-nav handler in speech-provider was still reading `slides[liveSlideIndex]` (= `slides[-1]` = `null`) to RECOVER the current passage's anchor (book+chapter+verse), and 3 of those handlers then COMMITTED the new verse via the legacy `setSlides + setPreviewSlideIndex + setLiveSlideIndex + setIsLive` combo (which clobbered the operator's preview pin and didn't actually update the AI's liveSlide ref). Net behaviour: "next verse" against an AI-detected live verse saw `null`, fell through to slide-deck fallback (or no-op); "next chapter" / "show verse N" reseated preview to the freshly-loaded verse but live stayed frozen on the original AI detection; "find_by_quote" yanked the operator's pinned preview off-screen.

**The eight problem sites and their fixes**:
- **L609 next_verse/previous_verse anchor read**: `const slide = liveIdx >= 0 ? slides[liveIdx] : null` → `const slide = s.liveSlide ?? (liveIdx >= 0 ? slides[liveIdx] : null)` (mirrors `logos-shell.tsx` L3929 AI-aware read).
- **L899-918 find_by_quote commit**: removed v0.7.194-hotfix.11 "preserve manual preview" guard (routed to `addScheduleItemQuiet` — silently sent the AI-found verse to schedule instead of live) + replaced `setSlides+setPreviewSlideIndex+setLiveSlideIndex+setIsLive` combo with `setLiveAuto(slide) + setLiveActiveVerseIndex(0)`.
- **L941 next_chapter/previous_chapter anchor read**: same liveSlide-first fix.
- **L989-1003 next_chapter/previous_chapter commit**: same v0.7.194 guard removal + setLiveAuto replacement.
- **L1136 show_verse_n anchor read**: same liveSlide-first fix.
- **L1204-1219 show_verse_n commit**: same v0.7.194 guard removal + setLiveAuto replacement.
- **L1482 LLM classifier context**: `const liveSlide = liveIdx >= 0 && liveIdx < slides.length ? slides[liveIdx] : undefined` → `const liveSlide = state.liveSlide ?? (...)`. Pre-214 the classifier received NO context when AI was driving live (liveIdx=-1), so "next verse" / "show verse 17" couldn't be resolved against the actual live passage.
- **L1110 delete_previous_verse**: dropped the `setPreviewSlideIndex(newIdx)` write — voice = LIVE only invariant; preview must never be reseated by a voice path.

**No change needed**: `change_translation` (L1078-1085) already only calls `setSelectedTranslation` and routes through the v0.7.208-fixed `live-translation-sync.tsx` watcher; `go_to_reference` (L747-785) was already on the `setLiveAuto` direct-ref path; `bible_says` (L1013-1054) intentionally writes preview only ("STANDBY only" — operator confirms with Enter / Go Live).

**New regression tests (7, extended `src/lib/ai-live-only.test.ts`)**: (a) BEHAVIOURAL — AI auto-detect Romans 8:16 via setLiveAuto (liveIdx=-1) + operator pins Genesis 1:1 to preview; voice next_verse simulated push of Romans 8:17 via setLiveAuto; assert `liveSlide.id === 'voice-jn3-17'`, `buildOutputPayload(state).slide.id === 'voice-jn3-17'`, AND preview untouched (`previewSlideIndex === 0`, `slides[0].id === 'op-gen'`, `slides.length === 1`). (b) GUARD source-grep on next_verse — `const slide = s.liveSlide ?? (liveIdx >= 0 ? slides[liveIdx] : null)` regex MUST match; pre-214 pattern MUST NOT appear. (c) GUARD on next_chapter case body — `setLiveAuto(slideNew)` present; `setPreviewSlideIndex(`, `setLiveSlideIndex(idx)`, `addScheduleItemQuiet(` all absent; liveSlide-first read pattern present. (d) GUARD on show_verse_n case body — same shape as next_chapter. (e) GUARD on find_by_quote case body — `setLiveAuto(slide)` present; legacy combo + addScheduleItemQuiet absent. (f) GUARD ZERO `setPreviewSlideIndex(` CALL SITES outside the bible_says case body — uses `setPreviewSlideIndex\(` regex (strict, excludes comment-text mentions) and an indexOf-bounded slice on the dispatcher's switch block. (g) GUARD on LLM classifier — anchored on the v0.7.214 comment marker, asserts `state.liveSlide ?? (liveIdx >= 0 ...)` pattern is present.

**Test results**: 16/16 PASS in `ai-live-only.test.ts` (was 9/9 on v0.7.208 — +7). Full suite **645/645 PASS** (was 638/638 on v0.7.213 — +7). 0 regressions.

**Replit runtime proof**: not separately scripted — test (a) exercises the EXACT store mutation the post-214 next_verse handler now performs (`setLiveAuto(stepped) + setLiveActiveVerseIndex(0)`), and the `buildOutputPayload` assertion mirrors the renderer's read path through the v0.7.203 output-payload contract. Guards (b)+(c)+(d)+(e)+(f)+(g) source-grep the file directly to prove the fix lives at the call sites (not in a wrapper that could be bypassed). Same hybrid proof shape as v0.7.208 / v0.7.210 / v0.7.212 / v0.7.213.

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — Every voice handler in `speech-provider.tsx` that reads the "current live passage anchor" MUST use `s.liveSlide ?? (liveIdx >= 0 ? slides[liveIdx] : null)`, NOT `slides[liveIdx]` alone**: the AI direct-ref path leaves liveSlideIndex at -1, so the bare-array read returns `null` and the handler degenerates to a slide-deck fallback that either no-ops or steps stale state. Tests (b)+(c)+(d) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — Every voice handler that COMMITS a freshly-loaded verse to live MUST use `setLiveAuto(slideNew) + setLiveActiveVerseIndex(0)`, NOT the legacy `setSlides + setPreviewSlideIndex + setLiveSlideIndex + setIsLive` combo**: the legacy combo clobbers operator preview (yanks pin away) AND fails to update the AI's liveSlide ref (so live freezes on the original detection). `setLiveAuto` is the canonical v0.7.203/v0.7.208/v0.7.210/v0.7.212 direct-ref primitive — same architecture every voice/AI path now shares. Tests (c)+(d)+(e) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — The voice dispatcher's switch body MUST contain ZERO `setPreviewSlideIndex(` CALL SITES outside the `bible_says` case**: bible_says is the ONLY voice command that legitimately writes preview ("STANDBY only" — operator confirms manually). Any other case calling `setPreviewSlideIndex(` re-opens this release's bug class. Test (f) regex `/setPreviewSlideIndex\(/g` with bibleSays-bounded slice enforces — counts calls, not comment-text mentions.

**GUARD-RAIL (D) — The LLM classifier MUST receive live-passage context derived from `state.liveSlide ?? slides[liveIdx]`, NOT `slides[liveIdx]` alone**: without the direct-ref read, the classifier sees no liveReference when AI is driving live, and "next verse" / "show verse 17" against an AI detection fall back to undefined context (cannot resolve). Test (g) anchored on the v0.7.214 comment marker enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL (E) — Voice handlers MUST NOT call `addScheduleItemQuiet` as a "preserve manual preview" fallback**: the v0.7.194-hotfix.11 pattern (route the loaded verse to the schedule when preview is off-live) silently routes operator-requested verses to the schedule instead of live, which is the exact OPPOSITE of what the voice command asked for. With `setLiveAuto` as the commit primitive, the preserve-manual-preview guard is unnecessary because `setLiveAuto` is inherently non-destructive to the preview state. Tests (c)+(d)+(e) `not.toMatch(/addScheduleItemQuiet\(/)` enforces.

**PROCESS GR — When a "fix N" introduces a new direct-ref primitive (`setLiveAuto`, `pinPreviewSlide`), the SAME-RELEASE audit MUST enumerate every reader/writer of the SHADOWED state, not just the headline call site**: v0.7.208 fixed `live-translation-sync.tsx` but didn't audit `speech-provider.tsx`'s 8 sites that read the same shadowed state. v0.7.210 introduced `pinPreviewSlide` and audited the symmetric writers in `setSlides` (v0.7.213) — but only after operator escalation. The right release-time question: `rg -n "<shadowed_field>" src/` THEN for each match classify (reader / writer / consumer) and verify the new primitive is consulted. This release's 8 sites were all in one file because they share the dispatcher pattern — but the principle is the same: one mental pass per release on every consumer of every primitive that touched the live/preview ownership model.

**PROCESS GR — Voice command tooltip phrasing ("apply to live" / "apply to preview" / "load into standby") MUST match the primitive call**: voice handlers whose intent says "apply to live" (next_verse, next_chapter, show_verse_n, find_by_quote, go_to_reference, change_translation, delete_previous_verse) MUST use `setLiveAuto` / `setLive*` family primitives. The ONE case whose intent says "load into standby" (bible_says) MUST use `setPreviewSlideIndex`. Any future voice command added to the dispatcher needs to declare its target at the top of the case body and the primitive call enforced by an `ai-live-only.test.ts` GUARD entry.
