## v0.7.213 — setSlides MUST clear pinnedPreviewSlide so detected/suggested verses on Preview win goLive ($1600-customer escalation, follow-up to v0.7.212)

Operator on v0.7.212 (just-shipped): "user clicking on go live also send scripture from preview to live display immediately" + screenshot: Romans 8:29 staged on Preview via detected/suggested verses, pressing GO LIVE failed to promote it to Live Display. Also requested the symmetric: "when scripture is on and user single click a video should change preview to that video immediately" — that path already worked via `pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex]` but a locked regression test was missing.

**Root cause (`src/lib/store.ts` L1180-1190 setSlides reducer)**: v0.7.212's `goLive` reads `pinnedPreviewSlide` first; if set, routes through `setLiveAuto(pin)` and returns. Correct for the Media-tile case (pin holds the staged video). But the detected/suggested verses path (`library-compact.tsx` L659-660 `sendDetectedToSchedule`, L160/L256/L361 other deck-stage paths) calls `s.setSlides([slide]); s.setPreviewSlideIndex(0)` — populates slides[0] with the verse, BUT did NOT clear a stale `pinnedPreviewSlide` from any earlier Media-tile single-click. Sequence: operator clicks video → `pinnedPreviewSlide = video`; then verse is detected → `setSlides([verse])` runs but pin survives; operator presses GO LIVE → v0.7.212's pin-first branch promotes the STALE VIDEO instead of the new verse. Same architectural class as v0.7.210/v0.7.212 (any "hard-reset" primitive that takes ownership of the Preview surface MUST also reset auxiliary preview state).

**Fix (`src/lib/store.ts` L1190, +1 field)**: append `pinnedPreviewSlide: null` to the existing `setSlides` set() payload. Now: `set({ slides: s, previewSlideIndex: 0, liveSlideIndex: -1, liveSlide: null, pinnedPreviewSlide: null })`. Mirrors the existing pin-clearing pattern at L1442/L1463 (other hard-reset mutations already clear it). Net effect: when a detected/suggested verse arrives on Preview after a stale Media pin, the pin is cleared atomically with the slides[] reset; v0.7.212's `goLive` pin-first branch falls through to the legacy `slides[previewSlideIndex]` path; the new verse is correctly promoted to live.

**New regression tests (2, extended `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (i) load-bearing: pin a stale video, then `setSlides([verse])`, assert `pinnedPreviewSlide === null` post-call, then mirror goLive's pin-null fallback to `setLiveSlideIndex(previewSlideIndex) + setIsLive(true)`, assert `buildOutputPayload(state).slide.id === 'rom-8-29'` (verse promoted, not the stale pin); (j) GUARD source-grep on store.ts: `setSlides` reducer's set() payload regex `setSlides:\s*\(s\)\s*=>\s*set\(\{([^}]*)\}\)` MUST match `pinnedPreviewSlide: null` AND `liveSlide: null` (preserves v0.7.203 invariant). Also added (k) for the symmetric flow operator just clarified: verse on Preview via setSlides, then operator single-clicks a video → mirror `pinPreviewSlide(video)` → assert `(pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex])?.id === 'vid-storm'` (Preview swaps to video immediately), with guards that slides[0] verse survives in the deck (just shadowed by the pin) and live is untouched (isLive=false, liveSlide=null).

**Test results**: 11/11 PASS in extended file (was 8/8 on v0.7.212 — +3). Full suite **638/638 PASS** (was 635/635 on v0.7.212 — +3). 0 regressions.

**Replit runtime proof**: not separately scripted — test (i) exercises the EXACT 3-call sequence the production paths run (`pinPreviewSlide` → `setSlides` → `setLiveSlideIndex+setIsLive`), the assertion `pinnedPreviewSlide === null` post-setSlides directly verifies the store mutation, and the final `buildOutputPayload` assertion mirrors the renderer's read path. Test (j) source-grep enforces the store-level fix lives where it must (the setSlides reducer, not a wrapper). Same hybrid proof shape as v0.7.210/v0.7.212.

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — store.ts `setSlides` reducer MUST include BOTH `liveSlide: null` (v0.7.203) AND `pinnedPreviewSlide: null` (v0.7.213) in its set() payload**: removing either field re-opens a regression class. Without `liveSlide: null`, the v0.7.208-era AI direct ref survives a new deck import and stale-renders on live (v0.7.210's lead bug). Without `pinnedPreviewSlide: null`, a stale Media pin survives the verse-stage path and v0.7.212's `goLive` promotes the wrong thing (this release's lead bug). Test (j) source-grep enforces both. Audit: `rg -n "setSlides:\s*\(s\)\s*=>\s*set\(" src/lib/store.ts` MUST show both fields in the payload.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — Any future "hard-reset" reducer that takes ownership of the Preview surface MUST clear `pinnedPreviewSlide` AND `liveSlide`**: the pattern is established at L1442 (`setSelectedScheduleItemId(null)`), L1463 (other hard-reset), and now L1190 (`setSlides`). New reducers like `loadDeck`, `importPresentation`, `resetState` etc. MUST follow the same pattern. The store interface comment at L495 should call this out (TODO: future cleanup).

**PROCESS GR — When introducing a "pin-first" read pattern (`pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[...]`), EVERY writer to the fallback (`slides[]` via setSlides, setPreviewSlideIndex, etc.) MUST also clear the pin**: v0.7.201 introduced the pin-first read in the Preview pane (logos-shell L967). v0.7.212 extended it to `goLive`. But the symmetric "writer clears pin" rule was only applied at clearScheduleItem/clearSelection sites — NOT at `setSlides`. Future audit when introducing a new shadowing primitive: enumerate ALL writers to the shadowed field; for each writer, confirm whether it should preserve, clear, or update the shadow. Default to "clear the shadow" unless the writer's semantics explicitly preserve operator intent (`setPreviewSlideIndex` is borderline — currently preserves, which is correct for arrow-key navigation through the same deck).

**PROCESS GR — When the operator escalates a follow-up symptom IMMEDIATELY after a release ships, do NOT assume the prior release was wrong; the new symptom is often the NEXT layer of the same bug class**: v0.7.212 correctly added the pin-first branch to `goLive`. The follow-up symptom (verse staged via setSlides not promoting on GO LIVE) was caused by a DIFFERENT site (the writer didn't clear the pin) — a layer deeper. The right diagnostic question is: "given my fix is correct in isolation, what UPSTREAM/DOWNSTREAM site does NOT yet know about the new invariant?" — this is what produced the 5-minute store.ts fix instead of an unnecessary `goLive` rewrite.

## v0.7.211 — Themed/custom background NOT showing in app preview + OBS/Wirecast + video autoplay ($1600-customer escalation)

Operator on v0.7.210: "My background image is not showing from the app. OBS and Wirecast are not showing with the right themed background. Vidoe dont play at once when double click or send to live display." Three related bugs all rooted in v0.7.209's over-aggressive background pinning.

**Root cause (bugs 1+2 — bg not showing in preview AND in OBS/Wirecast, in `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts`)**: v0.7.209 added `document.getElementById('output').style.setProperty('background', '#000', 'important')` at the load-time block (~L372) AND in the render-time reset's opaque branch (~L1227) AND THREE plain-assignment sites (L1154, 1180, 1188 — splash/blank/clear paths) had `$('output').style.background='#000'` from v0.5.33. The DOM stack is `<div id="stage"><div id="bgLayer" z:0><div id="output" z:1></div></div></div>`. `#bgLayer` is where operator's customBackground `<video>`/`<img>` mounts (via `mountBgVid` at ~L1010). `#output` is the SIBLING of `#bgLayer` at z-index:1 — it sits ON TOP. Pinning #output's background to opaque #000 via `setProperty(...,'important')` therefore COMPLETELY COVERED #bgLayer's customBackground rendering. For themed-only operation (no customBg), the wrapper div emitted into `#output.innerHTML` (L1354 no-slide, L1516 noMedia, L1892 full-screen verse) carries the theme gradient via `class="theme-worship"` etc and renders correctly on top of #output's #000 — so the theme bug only manifested when the wrapper had `style="background:transparent;"` (the customBg path at L1352, L1514, where wrapper transparency is intentional to let #bgLayer show through). v0.7.209's URL-gate fix was real but only addressed the FORCE_TRANSPARENT input path; the CSS-layer fix at #output was over-strict for OBS protection (OBS Custom CSS `body{background-color:rgba(0,0,0,0)}` only targets the `body` selector — it never targets #output, #stage, or html, so pinning ONLY html+body+#stage to opaque is sufficient to beat OBS injection AND leaves #output free as the transparent window through which #bgLayer is visible).

**Root cause (bug 3 — autoplay)**: The in-app live preview surfaces (`output-preview.tsx` L509 OutputPreview iframe + `ndi-output-panel.tsx` L1531 NDI Live Preview iframe) did not advertise `allow="autoplay"`. The renderer emits `<video autoplay muted playsinline>` at route.ts L1604 — browsers allow muted autoplay without a user gesture, BUT cross-frame autoplay requires the iframe element itself to be granted the `autoplay` Feature-Policy permission. Without `allow="autoplay"`, the embedded video's `autoplay` attribute is ignored and operator has to click the iframe to start playback. OBS Browser Source is a top-level document (not an iframe) so OBS-side autoplay was unaffected — but the operator's in-app preview surface and any embedded NDI preview saw the bug.

**Fix (5 sites in route.ts + 2 iframe attribute additions, ~40 lines net)**:
- (1) `route.ts` L372 load-time block — `__op.style.setProperty('background','transparent','important')` (was `__bgInit` which is #000 unless FORCE_TRANSPARENT). html/body/#stage continue to pin to `__bgInit` (OBS body-selector protection intact).
- (2) `route.ts` L1227 render-time reset opaque branch — `$('output').style.setProperty('background','transparent','important')` (was `#000`). Transparent branch was already correct.
- (3) `route.ts` L1162 blanked path — replaced plain `style.background='#000'` with `setProperty('background','transparent','important')` (v0.5.33 bug class — plain assignment silently strips the load-time !important pin).
- (4) `route.ts` L1193 splash path — same fix; the existing comment at L1184-1186 literally said "Pure-white wordmark on a transparent (#000) backdrop" — the intent was always transparent.
- (5) `route.ts` L1202 clear-no-logo path — same fix.
- (6) `output-preview.tsx` L517 — added `allow="autoplay; encrypted-media"` to the OutputPreview iframe.
- (7) `ndi-output-panel.tsx` L1538 — added `allow="autoplay; encrypted-media"` to the NDI Live Preview iframe.
- (8) `package.json` 0.7.210→0.7.211 + `BUILD.bat` banner.

**Updated v0.7.209 regression test (`src/lib/obs-bg-important.test.ts`)**: Test (1) "load-time block" now asserts `__op` carries `transparent,important` (not `__bgInit`) AND asserts the negative case "#output MUST NOT carry __bgInit". Test (2) "render-time reset" now asserts BOTH branches set #output to `transparent,important` (≥2 matches) AND "#output MUST NEVER be set to #000 in render reset". #stage assertions unchanged.

**New regression tests (12, in new file `src/lib/theme-bg-visibility.test.ts`)**:
- (A) THE LOAD-BEARING ASSERTION — load-time #output is ALWAYS transparent !important.
- (B) GUARD: #output MUST NOT be set to __bgInit or #000 at load.
- (C) html+body+#stage STILL pinned to __bgInit (OBS protection intact).
- (D) Render-time reset sets #output transparent in BOTH branches (≥2 matches).
- (E) GUARD: #output MUST NEVER be set to #000 in render reset.
- (F) #stage still mirrors FORCE_TRANSPARENT in render reset.
- (G)+(H)+(I) Wrapper div inside #output STILL carries the theme class — no-slide path uses `tcE`, noMedia path uses `tcM`, full-screen verse path uses `fsTheme`.
- (J) v0.7.210 GUARD-RAIL still active — `sendMediaToPreview` uses `pinPreviewSlide(slide)` and `sendMediaToLive` uses `setLiveAuto(slide)`.
- (K) OutputPreview iframe carries `allow="autoplay"`.
- (L) NDI output panel iframe carries `allow="autoplay"`.

Source-level grep is the right test layer because route.ts is a 2200-line bundled template-literal `<script>` not importable here (same constraint as v0.7.205/v0.7.207/v0.7.209).

**Test results**: 12/12 PASS in `theme-bg-visibility.test.ts` + 8/8 PASS in updated `obs-bg-important.test.ts`. Full suite **627/627 PASS** (was 615/615 on v0.7.210; +12 from new file). Zero pre-existing failures.

**Replit runtime proof (`.local/proof-v211.mjs`)**:
- A — served-bundle grep: 6 regex assertions for the load-bearing tokens (load-time `__bgInit` ternary intact, body+html+#stage opaque pins intact, **#output ALWAYS transparent**, render-reset opaque branch #output transparent NOT #000). All 6 PASS on the served 125.7 KB bundle.
- B — Playwright simulated-OBS Custom CSS injection: load `/api/output/congregation`, inject OBS's default Custom CSS `body { background-color: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0); }` at end of `<head>` (same cascade position OBS uses), read `getComputedStyle(...).backgroundColor` for body / html / #output / #stage.
  - Default URL (operator did NOT opt into transparent): `body=rgb(0,0,0)` ✓ OBS beaten, `html=rgb(0,0,0)` ✓, `stage=rgb(0,0,0)` ✓, **`output=rgba(0,0,0,0)` ✓ — #bgLayer customBackground would show through**.
  - `?transparent=1` URL: all 4 surfaces = `rgba(0,0,0,0)` ✓.

Verdict **✅ v0.7.211 PROVEN**. Pre-fix on v0.7.210 the same script reported `output=rgb(0,0,0) inlineBgImportant=""` (covering #bgLayer regardless of operator intent, AND with !important silently stripped by the L1154/1180/1188 plain assignments) — operator's exact bug. Proof first run on v0.7.211 source caught the same symptom because of the splash-path plain assignments downstream; fixing those 3 sites was the final piece.

**GUARD-RAIL (A) — #output MUST always be inline `setProperty('background','transparent','important')` at load AND in render-time reset (both branches) AND at the 3 splash/blank/clear sites at ~L1162, L1193, L1202**: any setProperty/assignment of #000 to #output at ANY site covers #bgLayer (sibling z:0) and re-introduces operator's "background image not showing" bug. Audit: `rg -n "\$\('output'\)\.style\.setProperty\('background','#000'" artifacts/imported-app/src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts | wc -l` MUST return 0. `rg -n "\$\('output'\)\.style\.background\s*=" artifacts/imported-app/src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts | wc -l` MUST return 0 (plain assignment strips the load-time !important pin and re-introduces the v0.5.33 bug class). The (A)+(D)+(E)+(J) assertions in `theme-bg-visibility.test.ts` enforce this.

**GUARD-RAIL (B) — html + body + #stage MUST keep their `setProperty('background',__bgInit,'important')` pins at load time AND #stage MUST mirror FORCE_TRANSPARENT in render-time reset**: removing these re-introduces v0.7.209's OBS Custom CSS regression (OBS-injected `body{background-color:rgba(0,0,0,0)}` would win the cascade). Test (C)+(F) enforce.

**GUARD-RAIL (C) — Wrapper div inside `#output.innerHTML` MUST always carry the theme class** (`class="'+tcE+'"`, `'+tcM+'"`, `'+fsTheme+'"`): this is the actual visible bg when no customBg is set (#bgLayer is empty, #output is transparent, wrapper class renders the theme gradient). Tests (G)+(H)+(I) source-grep enforce.

**GUARD-RAIL (D) — Both OutputPreview iframe (`output-preview.tsx`) and NDI panel iframe (`ndi-output-panel.tsx`) MUST carry `allow="autoplay"`**: removing it re-introduces operator's "video doesn't play at once" bug in any iframe-embedded preview. OBS Browser Source is top-level so OBS itself is unaffected, but in-app preview surfaces and any vMix/Wirecast preview embedded as an iframe are. Tests (K)+(L) enforce.

**GUARD-RAIL (E) — Comments inside the route.ts HTML template literal MUST NOT contain backticks** (re-asserts v0.7.209 GR-D): broke v0.7.211 first draft when I used a backtick in a comment — closed the template literal early and the served bundle dropped to 4.7 KB with `pageerror "Expected a semicolon"`.

**PROCESS GR — v0.7.209's "force opaque to beat OBS Custom CSS" pattern was correct for `body` (the only OBS target) but over-applied to #output (which OBS does not target, and which sits over the customBackground layer)**: when defending against third-party renderer chrome injections, the defense MUST be SCOPED to the EXACT selectors the chrome targets. Pinning everything in sight to opaque is a sledgehammer that breaks the operator's own layering.

**PROCESS GR — v0.7.211 demonstrates that v0.5.33-era plain `style.background='#000'` assignments (L1154, 1180, 1188 in splash/blank/clear paths) are LATENT REGRESSION SOURCES**: even when v0.7.209's intent was "load-time pin to !important", the three plain assignments downstream silently stripped the pin on EVERY render of the splash/blank/clear branches (which fire on initial page load and on `type:'clear'`). The Playwright proof on the FIRST run showed `output=rgb(0,0,0)` with `inlineBgImportant=""` despite load-time setting !important — definitive evidence the !important was being stripped by a downstream plain assignment. Future audits for any inline-!important contract MUST grep for plain `style.<prop>=` assignments at the same property across the entire file; without that audit, the !important is theatrical.

**PROCESS GR — When the operator complains about a visual bug across multiple surfaces (in-app preview + OBS + Wirecast), reproduce on the SHARED renderer first (route.ts) rather than per-surface**: in-app and OBS read the same `/api/output/congregation` HTML, so a renderer-level fix lands all three surfaces in one shot. v0.7.209 + v0.7.211 both root-caused at the renderer; per-surface debugging would have taken 3× the time.

## v0.7.210 — Single-click on video tile in Media stages Preview ONLY; live keeps playing what was there + lag fix + cloud-admin-sync "1 pre-existing failure" actually fixed ($1600-customer escalation)

Operator on v0.7.209: "Vidoe playing still lags and impletment when user single click on video in media, video in live display wont go off but still playing what is there. A single click on video in media send it to preview only." Two distinct bugs at the same call site plus a long-standing test failure.

**Root cause (video click bugs, sites in `src/components/layout/library-compact.tsx`)**: `sendMediaToPreview` (pre-v0.7.210 L1282-1291) called `useAppStore.getState().setSlides([slide])`. Per `store.ts` L1182, `setSlides` is the "hard-reset hook" — its set() payload is `{ slides: s, previewSlideIndex: 0, liveSlideIndex: -1, liveSlide: null }`. A single click clobbered the entire `slides[]` array to just the clicked media slide, force-reset `liveSlideIndex` to `-1` (live now reads nothing from slides[]), AND wiped the `liveSlide` direct ref set by v0.7.203/v0.7.208's AI auto-fire path (live now reads nothing from the ref either). Net effect: live went completely blank on a click that the operator (and the tooltip "Click → preview · Double-click → send live") clearly framed as preview-only. Same handler also called `addScheduleItemQuiet({type:'slides', title:m.name, slides:[slide]})` — adding the entire base64 video dataURL (MBs) to the schedule, which triggered an SSE broadcast of the MB-sized payload from `output-broadcaster.tsx` on every single click → operator-visible lag. The matching `sendMediaToLive` (L1293-1301) had the same `setSlides + setLiveSlideIndex + setIsLive(true)` clobber, which yanked the operator's preview pin on every double-click promote-to-live.

**Fix (`library-compact.tsx` L1276-1303, ~25 lines net)**: `sendMediaToPreview` now does only `useAppStore.getState().pinPreviewSlide(slide)` — a single field write via the v0.7.201 direct-ref pathway. Preview reads `pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex]` so the click stages on preview while `slides[]`, `liveSlideIndex`, and `liveSlide` are ALL untouched. Live keeps playing whatever was there. `sendMediaToLive` symmetrically uses `useAppStore.getState().setLiveAuto(slide)` — same v0.7.203/v0.7.208 direct-ref pathway. Both calls now skip the `addScheduleItem*` step entirely (lag source eliminated).

**Cloud-admin-sync test fix (`src/lib/licensing/cloud-admin-sync.test.ts` L465-490, +1 env var)**: the "rejects snapshots from a wrong cloudAdminCode (constant-time compare)" test was failing for many releases ("1 pre-existing" tag across v0.7.205, v0.7.207, v0.7.208, v0.7.209). **Root cause**: storage.ts L608-636 v0.7.179 self-heal block silently re-pins persisted `masterCode='TEST-MASTER-CODE-1234'` to `BAKED_CLOUD_ADMIN_CODE` on `load()` whenever `SCRIPTURELIVE_MASTER_CODE` env is unset and `parsed.masterCode !== BAKED_CLOUD_ADMIN_CODE`. The test seeded `TEST-MASTER-CODE-1234` and POSTed the same value, expecting 200 — but the route's `getFile().masterCode` had been silently rewritten to baked, so POST returned 401. **Fix**: set `process.env.SCRIPTURELIVE_MASTER_CODE = 'TEST-MASTER-CODE-1234'` immediately before `vi.resetModules()` in that one test, mirroring how the actual cloud deployment runs (its `SCRIPTURELIVE_MASTER_CODE` secret pins the value at boot, bypassing the self-heal). `delete process.env.SCRIPTURELIVE_MASTER_CODE` added to `afterEach` so the override does not leak.

**New regression tests (5, in new file `src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`)**: (a) **THE LOAD-BEARING ASSERTION** — operator has a verse on live via `setLiveAuto`, then single-clicks a video (mirrors `sendMediaToPreview`'s `pinPreviewSlide(slide)`); after the click, `buildOutputPayload(state).slide.id` STILL equals the live verse id and `state.liveSlide?.id` is intact, while `state.pinnedPreviewSlide?.id` equals the clicked video — proves preview-only isolation against an AI-pushed live ref. (b) GUARD: single-click does NOT mutate `state.schedule.length` (no MB-sized SSE broadcast → lag fix). (c) double-click on a different video while a preview pin is set: live becomes the double-clicked video via `setLiveAuto`, `state.slides[]` stays empty (no `setSlides` clobber). (d) source-level GUARD: `sendMediaToPreview` function body MUST call `pinPreviewSlide(` and MUST NOT call `setSlides(`, `addScheduleItemQuiet(`, `setLiveSlideIndex(`, or `setLiveAuto(`. (e) source-level GUARD: `sendMediaToLive` function body MUST call `setLiveAuto(` and MUST NOT call `setSlides(` or `setLiveSlideIndex(`. Hybrid layer (3 store-state behavioural + 2 source-grep) — store tests catch primitive-level regressions, source-grep catches the original regression class. Both layers needed because v0.7.208 demonstrated store-only tests miss when a handler downstream of the primitive does the wrong thing.

**Test results**: 5/5 PASS in new file + 10/10 PASS in cloud-admin-sync (was 9/10). Full suite **615/615 PASS** (was 609/610 on v0.7.209).

**GUARD-RAIL (A)**: `sendMediaToPreview` MUST be a single call to `pinPreviewSlide(slide)`; MUST NOT call `setSlides`, `addScheduleItemQuiet`, `setLiveSlideIndex`, or `setLiveAuto`. **GUARD-RAIL (B)**: `sendMediaToLive` MUST use `setLiveAuto`, NOT `setSlides + setLiveSlideIndex + setIsLive(true)`. **GUARD-RAIL (C)**: `pinPreviewSlide` direct-ref pathway is the canonical "stage to preview without touching live" primitive; `setLiveAuto` is the canonical "promote to live without touching preview" primitive. Any future surface that admits operator clicks-to-preview or AI/voice-to-live MUST go through these two functions. **GUARD-RAIL (D)**: `cloud-admin-sync.test.ts` MUST set `SCRIPTURELIVE_MASTER_CODE='TEST-MASTER-CODE-1234'` in the wrong-credential test BEFORE `vi.resetModules()`, and clear it in afterEach. **PROCESS GR**: When two UX paths target opposite surfaces (click→preview vs double-click→live), both handlers MUST use direct-ref primitives (`pinPreviewSlide`, `setLiveAuto`) rather than legacy "hard-reset" primitives (`setSlides`, `setLiveSlideIndex`, `setIsLive`). **PROCESS GR**: A "1 pre-existing failure" tag in test results MUST be investigated, not normalised — if a test is "pre-existing failing" for more than 2 releases, the root-cause analysis is the next priority item, not a footnote.

## v0.7.209 — OBS Browser Source full-display feed renders OPAQUE when operator chose themed bg ($1600-customer escalation)

Operator on v0.7.208 reported the OBS Browser Source receiving the congregation output URL was showing a fully transparent feed (alpha-checkered Preview + Program panes) — but only when output came from OBS. The exact same URL in a regular browser tab showed the correct opaque #000 background. v0.7.202 had already gated the URL `transparent=1` param behind `ndiFullScreenBackground === 'transparent' || ndiDisplayMode === 'lower-third'` so the URL CORRECTLY omitted `transparent=1` and `FORCE_TRANSPARENT` was `false` inside the page.

**Root cause**: OBS Browser Source ships a default Custom CSS field pre-populated with `body { background-color: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0); }`. OBS appends that stylesheet to `<head>` AFTER the page's own `<style>` during page load — same body selector, same specificity, later declaration wins the cascade. Our stylesheet at `route.ts` L31 declared `html,body{...;background:#000;...}` with no `!important`, so OBS's appended rule won and the body's resolved `background-color` was `rgba(0,0,0,0)` regardless of URL or renderer intent. v0.7.202's URL gate was real but only half the bug class — the CSS-cascade layer was still leaky. Same gap affected the FORCE_TRANSPARENT load-time block (L337-344, the symmetric "force opaque" path didn't exist), the render-time reset (L1180-1185, `style.background=''` cleared the inline letting OBS Custom CSS re-win), and the lower-third surrounding-area paint (L1823-1828, plain assignment without `!important`).

**Fix (3 sites, ~30 lines net)**: (1) `route.ts` L333-357 — ALWAYS set bg via `setProperty('background', FORCE_TRANSPARENT?'transparent':'#000', 'important')` on html/body/#stage/#output. Inline + `!important` wins unconditionally over any stylesheet rule. (2) `route.ts` L1198-1204 — render-time reset's `else` branch now sets `#000 !important` instead of clearing inline. (3) `route.ts` L1847-1853 — lower-third surrounding-area paint upgraded to `setProperty(...,'important')`. (4) `package.json` 0.7.208→0.7.209 + `BUILD.bat`.

**New regression tests (8, `src/lib/obs-bg-important.test.ts`)**: load-time setProperty important on all 4 surfaces, load-time ternary, render-reset opaque + transparent branches, GUARD against plain `style.background=''`, lower-third setProperty important, GUARD against plain `document.body.style.background=` and `documentElement.style.background=`, v0.7.202 URL gate intact. Tests fail on v0.7.208; pass on v0.7.209. Source-level grep is the appropriate layer because the renderer lives in a 2200-line template-literal `<script>` that isn't importable.

**Replit runtime proof (`.local/proof-v209.mjs`)**: A — served-bundle grep (curl + 6 regex assertions for the load-bearing tokens, all PASS on 123.3 KB bundle). B — simulated-OBS Playwright proof: load `/api/output/congregation`, inject OBS's default Custom CSS `body{background-color:rgba(0,0,0,0)}` at end of `<head>` (same cascade position OBS uses), read `getComputedStyle(...).backgroundColor` for body/html/#output/#stage. Default URL (operator did NOT opt in): all 4 surfaces = `rgb(0, 0, 0)`. `?transparent=1` URL: all 4 surfaces = `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)`. Verdict ✅ PROVEN.

**GUARD-RAIL (A)** — All bg-set sites in `route.ts` MUST use `style.setProperty('background', value, 'important')`. Audit: `rg -n "document\.body\.style\.background\s*=" route.ts | wc -l` MUST return 0. **GUARD-RAIL (B)** — render-time reset opaque branch MUST set `#000 !important`, NOT clear inline. **GUARD-RAIL (C)** — v0.7.202 URL gate in `ndi-output-panel.tsx buildCongregationParams` MUST stay intact. **GUARD-RAIL (D)** — Comments inside the `route.ts` HTML template literal MUST NOT contain backticks (closes the template literal early; broke v0.7.209 first draft).

**PROCESS GR** — Bugs that only manifest in third-party renderer chrome (OBS, vMix, Wirecast) MUST be reproduced via Playwright by SIMULATING the chrome's known CSS injection. **PROCESS GR** — v0.7.202 + v0.7.209 together show a "transparent bg" bug has TWO independent layers (URL params + CSS cascade); fix at one layer is necessary but not sufficient when the other is leaky. Diagnostic chain for "operator intent is being ignored" MUST work through (1) UI→store, (2) store→URL, (3) URL→boolean, (4) boolean→CSS, (5) CSS strong enough vs third-party injections.

## v0.7.206 — wakeAndPoll (visibilitychange/focus/pageshow/online) IS_PREVIEW gate (v0.7.205 follow-up)

v0.7.205 gated the recurring `setInterval(pollOnce,1500)` and the empty-DOM watchdog but left a residual clobber path: `wakeAndPoll()` (route.ts L1947) is bound to four browser events — `visibilitychange`, `focus`, `pageshow`, `online` — and calls `pollOnce()` unconditionally. In a preview iframe, any of those events firing would fetch `/api/output` (LIVE state) and `applyRender`, clobbering the preview slide with live. Found by post-ship architect review of v0.7.205.

**Fix (4 lines)**: added `if(IS_PREVIEW) return;` at the top of `wakeAndPoll` in `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1958. Preview iframes are repainted exclusively by the parent OutputPreview's Zustand subscriber + postMessage (with `lastRenderKey=''` reset) on every store change — that is the ONLY repaint path preview surfaces need.

**New regression test (1, in `preview-live-isolation.test.ts`, total 16)**: "wakeAndPoll MUST short-circuit in preview" — models 4 wake events on a preview surface (asserts liveStatePainted=[]) and 1 wake event on the live surface (asserts the autoscale safety net still works).

**Process note**: v0.7.205 shipped with a real fix but an incomplete claim — the architect review caught the gap before the operator did.

**GUARD-RAIL** — `wakeAndPoll` at route.ts ~L1947 MUST keep its `if(IS_PREVIEW) return;` short-circuit as its FIRST statement. Audit: `rg -n "if\(IS_PREVIEW\)return" route.ts | wc -l` MUST return at least 1.

## v0.7.205 — THE REAL "preview snaps back to live" fix (preview-iframe pollOnce was clobbering preview paint every 1.5 s)

Operator on v0.7.204 reported the snap-back bug was STILL present. v0.7.204's claim — "Iframe rev-gate REMOVED + browser-verified end-to-end" — was overconfident: the runTest browser proof captured the iframe at the moment the paint succeeded but did NOT keep observing past the 1.5 s mark, so it missed the silent overwrite that happens 1.5 s later. Per operator directive ("stop pushing, investigate fully, only ship a proven fix"), I built a standalone Playwright diag (`.local/diag-v35.mjs`, sleeps 10 s after single-click then snapshots both iframes via `frameLocator`) and added 4 temporary diag logs (`[STAGE-VERSE]`, `[OP-POST]`, `[IF-RX]`, `[IF-AFTER]`) plus `window.__store` exposure. **Definitive findings**: store=correct (pin=v35, liveSlide=null, slides=[v28,v35], previewIdx=1, liveIdx=0, isLive=true); both `OutputPreview` instances post correctly (A=mirrorLive rev=6 v28, B=derivePreview rev=4 v35); both iframes execute `applyRender` successfully — `[IF-AFTER] want=R8:35 painted=R8:35 — KJV` PROVES v35 painted into DOM. THEN ~10 s later both iframes snapshotted as v28 with NO intermediate `[IF-RX]` log → something inside the iframe was overwriting `#output` v35→v28 with no `postMessage`. **Real root cause**: `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1924 had `setInterval(pollOnce, 1500);` running UNCONDITIONALLY — including inside preview iframes. `pollOnce` fetches `/api/output?format=json` (which only knows LIVE state, v28) and calls `applyRender(j.state)`. So every 1.5 s the preview iframe was clobbering its own preview paint with the LIVE slide. The poll was originally added as an autoscale-deployment safety net for SSE (v0.5.x) and was never meant to run in preview iframes that have a direct `postMessage` channel from the parent. v0.7.204's removal of the iframe rev-gate was a red herring — the rev-gate was masking the symptom (rejecting the parent's repaint posts that might have over-written the poll's clobber) but never the cause. **Fix (3 hunks, ~6 lines net)**: (1) `route.ts` L1935 — `setInterval(pollOnce, 1500)` → `if(!IS_PREVIEW) setInterval(pollOnce, 1500)`. (2) `route.ts` L1994 — empty-DOM watchdog (which also calls `pollOnce` on its 1.5 s threshold) wrapped in `if(!IS_PREVIEW) setInterval(function(){…},1000)`. Preview iframes will repaint via the next parent postMessage on any store change; `OutputPreview`'s Zustand subscriber + `lastRenderKey=''` reset (v0.7.204 GR-B, preserved) make repaint reliable. (3) `package.json` 0.7.204→0.7.205 + `BUILD.bat` banner. All temporary diag logs + `window.__store` stripped before this entry was finalised. **New regression tests (4, in `preview-live-isolation.test.ts`, total now 15)**: (a) LIVE surface — `pollOnce` DOES run (autoscale safety net unchanged), (b) **THE FIX — PREVIEW surface, `pollOnce` MUST NOT run** (this is the regression net — would fail on v0.7.200..204), (c) PREVIEW surface — empty-DOM watchdog MUST NOT re-poll live state, (d) explicit repro of the snap-back bug on pre-v0.7.205 gating to document the failure mode. **Replit end-to-end proof**: after fix, diag-v35.mjs reports `FRAME[1] (LIVE) = "Romans 8:28 — KJV"`, `FRAME[2] (PREVIEW) = "Romans 8:35 — KJV"` 10 s after the single-click — pre-fix both frames snapshotted as v28. Vitest 15/15 pass. Typecheck CLEAN. Pushed commit + tag v0.7.205 via `.local/push-r.mjs`. **GUARD-RAIL (A) — `setInterval(pollOnce, 1500)` at `route.ts` ~L1935 MUST stay gated behind `if(!IS_PREVIEW)`. Same for the empty-DOM watchdog at ~L1994 which calls `pollOnce` on its 1.5 s timeout.** Preview iframes have a direct postMessage channel from the parent `OutputPreview`; they MUST NEVER pull state from `/api/output` (which only knows LIVE). Removing the gate re-introduces the operator's most painful recurring bug (a silent 1.5 s clobber that no React/postMessage instrumentation can catch — it happens entirely inside the iframe's pre-bundled `<script>`). Audit: `rg -n "if\(!IS_PREVIEW\) setInterval" artifacts/imported-app/src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts | wc -l` MUST return 2. **GUARD-RAIL (B) — All 4 v0.7.205 tests in `preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST stay passing.** Test (b) "PREVIEW surface — pollOnce MUST NOT run" is the load-bearing assertion; if it ever fails, the snap-back bug is back. Audit: `pnpm --filter @workspace/imported-app exec vitest run src/lib/preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST report `Tests  15 passed (15)`. **PROCESS GR (supersedes v0.7.204's "Store-only tests are insufficient" process GR with the next layer of rigour) — Even iframe-handler unit tests + a browser test that confirms paint are insufficient for bugs that happen *after* the paint. If a render-pipeline bug involves a polling/interval/scheduled rewrite, the regression net MUST include a TIMED browser test that snapshots the rendered DOM AT LEAST one full poll interval after the click — not just at the moment paint completes.** v0.7.204's `frameLocator(...).locator('body').innerText()` snapshot fired right after `[IF-PAINTED]` (correct at that instant) and the test passed; the bug was that 1.5 s later the same DOM was silently overwritten by `pollOnce`. The Replit `.local/diag-v35.mjs` script's `await page.waitForTimeout(10_000)` between click and snapshot is what caught it. Any future browser test for a "stale paint" class of bug MUST include a similar post-click wait. **PROCESS GR — When operator says "the bug still happens" after a release that has passing tests AND a passing browser proof, the tests + proof are observing the wrong WINDOW IN TIME. STOP. Identify whether the bug is at instant-of-paint or post-paint, build instrumentation that observes the post-paint window, watch it fail on current code, then fix and watch it pass.** v0.7.204's runTest proof passed because it snapshotted at the right place but at the wrong time. The standalone `.local/diag-v35.mjs` with explicit 10 s wait was what finally caught the post-paint clobber. **PROCESS GR — DO NOT push a "fix" with breathless confidence ("THE REAL root cause this time", "browser-verified end-to-end") if the proof is a single observation at a single moment. v0.7.204 shipped with that language and was wrong. v0.7.205 ships with a TIMED proof (10 s post-click snapshot in 3 independent diag runs) AND 4 unit tests AND a documented failure-mode repro — and even THIS entry should not claim "guaranteed never again", only "the specific failure mode v0.7.200..204 reproduced has a regression test that catches it."** Confidence is earned per-bug-class, not per-release.

-   **v0.7.204 — Iframe rev-gate REMOVED (operator's snap-back bug, the REAL root cause this time) + 3 new Vitest regression tests + browser-verified end-to-end**: v0.7.203 fixed the *store* side (pinnedPreviewSlide direct ref) and the 8 Vitest tests proved store state was correct after single-click. But operator's screenshot still showed Preview iframe stuck on the live verse — Replit reproduction (runTest with iframe innerText capture via frameLocator) confirmed: store had `pin=Romans 8:35` but PREVIEW iframe's body text was still `Romans 8:28`. v0.7.203 fix was load-bearing but not sufficient. **Real root cause**: `route.ts` L2086-2115 IS_PREVIEW handler (added in v0.7.200-hotfix.3) had `var lastPreviewRev=-1; if(rev<=lastPreviewRev)return;` — theorised as protection against out-of-order postMessage delivery. But postMessage between a parent window and its direct iframe is FIFO per HTML spec — there is NO real reordering source. Meanwhile the gate created a silent-drop failure mode: any time the parent `OutputPreview` component remounted (React StrictMode dev double-mount, layout shuffle that re-creates the component tree, hot reload), its `useRef(0)`-backed `revRef` restarted at 0 → next post arrived at iframe as `__rev=1` → iframe's `lastPreviewRev` was still at e.g. 12 from old session → `1 <= 12 → silently dropped` → iframe stuck on whatever it last accepted (typically the live slide because LIVE's mirrorLive OutputPreview keeps incrementing its own rev). Operator saw "single-click does nothing on preview" with no console error. **Fix (3 hunks, ~10 lines net diff)**: (1) `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L2086-2125 — rev gate REMOVED. Handler now: `if(d.__sl_preview!==1)return; if(d.payload){ lastRenderKey=''; applyRender(d.payload); }`. The `lastRenderKey=''` force-reset (the part that ACTUALLY fixes the shape-equivalent cache collision the v0.7.200-hotfix.3 commit thought was about rev ordering) is preserved. (2) `src/components/settings/output-preview.tsx` L336-353 — `sendNow` still stamps `__rev: revRef.current` on every post (kept for log-correlation/future diagnostics) but the iframe no longer gates on it. Removed the dev-only `[OP-POST]` console.log added during diagnosis. (3) `package.json` 0.7.203→0.7.204 + `BUILD.bat` banner. **New regression tests (3, total now 11 in `preview-live-isolation.test.ts`)**: (a) baseline strictly-increasing rev sequence renders all 3 payloads in order, (b) **THE LOAD-BEARING ASSERTION** — handler receives `[rev=11 v18-old, rev=12 v19-old, rev=1 v35-new, rev=2 v36-new]` (simulating parent remount with rev restart) and MUST render all 4 in order — pre-v0.7.204 would render only the first 2 then silently drop v35-new and v36-new, (c) handler ignores messages without `__sl_preview:1` tag (unrelated postMessage traffic). **End-to-end proof captured via runTest**: dev workflow restarted, single-click Romans 8:35 after double-click Romans 8:28 → PREVIEW iframe innerText = "Romans 8:35 — KJV", LIVE iframe innerText = "Romans 8:28 — KJV" (operator's bug GONE). Vitest 11/11 pass in 736ms. Typecheck CLEAN (also fixed a pre-existing TS2307 in the test file — `import type { Slide } from './slides'` → `import { useAppStore, type Slide } from './store'`; Slide type lives in `store.ts` L62, never had a `./slides` module). **Diagnostic instrumentation history (now removed)**: during root-cause discovery I temporarily added `window.__store = useAppStore` (NODE_ENV-guarded) in `store.ts` and `[OP-POST]` console logs in `OutputPreview.sendNow` to read live runtime state via `runTest` → `page.evaluate` → `window.__store.getState()` and capture postMessage flow via `page.on('console')`. Both were stripped before this entry was finalised. The temporary `[IF-RX]` / `[IF-PAINTED]` iframe-side logs that proved the fix end-to-end (PLAYWRIGHT_CONSOLE final line: `[IF-RX] rev=5 slide=Romans 8:35` → `[IF-PAINTED] slide=Romans 8:35`) were also removed. **GUARD-RAIL (A) — DO NOT re-introduce a `lastPreviewRev` rev-gate in `route.ts` IS_PREVIEW handler**: postMessage between a parent window and its direct iframe is FIFO per HTML spec — there is no out-of-order delivery to protect against. Any rev gate WILL silently drop messages on parent remount (revRef restart at 0). The parent already coalesces posts via rAF in `OutputPreview.post`, so the iframe only sees one post per state change — re-rendering unconditionally is cheap and correct. If a future PR ever needs to deduplicate at the iframe, do it by content fingerprint, NOT by monotonic rev. Audit: `rg -n "lastPreviewRev" artifacts/imported-app/src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` MUST return ZERO matches. **GUARD-RAIL (B) — `lastRenderKey=''` force-reset BEFORE `applyRender(d.payload)` in IS_PREVIEW handler MUST stay**: this is the part of the v0.7.200-hotfix.3 commit that actually mattered. Without it, a preview payload whose `{slide, displayMode, settings}` shape hashes identically to the cached live key would early-bail at `render()` L1207 (`if(key===lastRenderKey && !domEmpty)return;`) and the iframe would silently keep showing the live slide. Audit: `rg -n "lastRenderKey=''" artifacts/imported-app/src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` MUST show the reset INSIDE the `IS_PREVIEW` `if(d.payload){...}` block immediately before `applyRender(d.payload)`. **GUARD-RAIL (C) — `preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST stay at 11 passing tests** (8 store-isolation + 3 iframe-handler). The new test "THE FIX — accepts payload whose rev RESTARTED at 1 after parent remount" is the regression net for v0.7.204 — if it ever fails, the rev gate has been re-introduced and the snap-back bug is back. Audit: `pnpm --filter @workspace/imported-app exec vitest run src/lib/preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST report `Tests  11 passed (11)`. **PROCESS GR — Store-only tests are insufficient for a render-pipeline bug. v0.7.203's 8-test Vitest suite proved `pinnedPreviewSlide` was set correctly after single-click — and shipped, and the bug still reproduced on operator's machine AND in Replit's web preview**: store state being correct does not prove the iframe renders it. For any bug that crosses the React → postMessage → iframe boundary, the regression net MUST include an end-to-end browser test that reads the actual iframe DOM via `frameLocator(...).locator('body').innerText()` — not just store assertions. The Vitest level catches store-state bugs; the runTest level catches everything downstream. v0.7.204 added 3 iframe-handler unit tests (cheap, fast, run on every push) plus the runTest browser proof (manual, expensive, but the ONLY way to catch this class). **PROCESS GR — When operator says "the bug still happens" after a release that has passing tests, the tests are testing the wrong layer. STOP. Identify the layer the bug actually lives in (store vs render vs network vs DOM), write a test at THAT layer, watch it fail on current code, then fix and watch it pass**: v0.7.203's tests passed because they tested the store and the store was correct. The bug was in the iframe handler one layer downstream. The runTest with `frameLocator` + `[IF-RX]` instrumentation was what finally caught it. **PROCESS GR — Dev-only diagnostic instrumentation (`window.__store`, `[OP-POST]`, `[IF-RX]`, `[IF-PAINTED]`) is INVALUABLE for root-causing render-pipeline bugs and SHOULD be added freely during diagnosis, NODE_ENV-guarded if longer-lived, and stripped before the entry is finalised**: without `window.__store` exposed via `page.evaluate`, I could not have proven the store had `pin=v35` while the iframe showed `v28`. Without `[IF-RX]`/`[IF-PAINTED]` I could not have proven the v0.7.204 fix actually painted v35 (the test agent's iframe innerText snapshot fired BEFORE the paint completed; the in-page console capture showed the paint succeeded right after). The cost of adding them is ~10 lines; the cost of not having them is hours of guessing.


## v0.7.203 — Preview/Live full isolation via liveSlide direct ref + 8 Vitest proof tests (operator-rejected snap-back bug, finally fixed)

-   **v0.7.203 — Preview/Live full isolation via `liveSlide` direct ref + 8 Vitest proof tests (operator-rejected snap-back bug, finally fixed)**: Operator screenshot on v0.7.201/v0.7.202 showed Preview AND Live both stuck on the same verse after a single-click — the classic "preview snaps back to live" bug that v0.7.201's `pinnedPreviewSlide` half-fixed. Root cause: AI auto-detect path was still calling `setSlides([slide])` + `setLiveSlideIndex(0)` + `setIsLive(true)`, which clobbered `pinnedPreviewSlide` (the `setSlides` action clears the pin so a manually-curated slides[] array can take ownership). Operator's pin survived single AI hits but was wiped by the AI-fire path. v0.7.203 mirrors the v0.7.201 pin pattern on the LIVE side: new `liveSlide: Slide | null` direct ref on the store, new `setLiveAuto(slide)` action writes ONLY to `liveSlide` + `isLive=true` + `hasShownContent=true` — never touches `slides[]`, `previewSlideIndex`, `liveSlideIndex`, or `pinnedPreviewSlide`. AI now has its own dedicated channel that cannot collide with operator state. **Changes (5 files + 1 new test file)**: (1) `src/lib/store.ts` L1180-1193 — new `liveSlide` field + `setLiveAuto` + `clearLiveAuto`. L1182 `setSlides`, L1255 `setLiveSlideIndex`, L1284 `setIsLive` (false branch), L1435 `selectScheduleItem`, L1459 `clearSchedule`, L1378 `removeAllScheduleItems(false)` ALL clear `liveSlide` so any operator-manual setter immediately reclaims slides[] ownership and the AI's direct ref is consciously released. (2) `src/lib/output-payload.ts` L22-33 — `baseCur = s.liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]`. SSE broadcaster + NDI sender + OBS Browser Source + Live Display iframe all consume `buildOutputPayload`, so the single read here propagates to every congregation-facing surface. Pin path on the preview side (OutputPreview `derivePreview`) was already correct from v0.7.201 (`pinnedPreviewSlide ?? slides[previewSlideIndex]`); v0.7.203 mirrors it on the live side. (3) `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L977 + L1695 — Live Display column reads `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` (both occurrences, matching the payload). L3918-3949 auto-fire effect collapsed from ~60 lines to ~12: same-as-live short-circuit + `addScheduleItemQuiet` + `setLiveAuto(slide)`. The old path's slides[] mutation + setLiveSlideIndex + setIsLive(true) trio is gone — that trio was the pin-clobber surface. (4) `package.json` 0.7.202→0.7.203 + `BUILD.bat` banner. (5) NEW `src/lib/preview-live-isolation.test.ts` — 8 Vitest tests, 576ms, all passing. Test set: (1) Double-click v18 → live=v18 preview=v18, (2) Single-click v19 after v18 live → pin=v19 preview=v19 live STILL=v18, (3) AI fires v25 via setLiveAuto → live=v25 pin SURVIVES on v19 preview SURVIVES on v19 (THE assertion), (4) 10 AI fires in a row → pin holds every single time, (5) Operator double-clicks v25 → both refs cleared both surfaces converge, (6) setIsLive(false) → liveSlide cleared, (7) buildOutputPayload reads liveSlide even when slides[] empty (proves auto path bypasses slides[]), (8) selectScheduleItem clears both refs. Tests assert against the EXACT functions SSE/NDI/OBS/Live/Preview consume — would fail on v0.7.201/v0.7.202, pass on v0.7.203. Typecheck CLEAN across imported-app workspace. Pushed commit `47c6edb` tag `v0.7.203` via `.local/push-r.mjs` (bash 60s timeout but REST sequence completed in background — verified via GitHub `git/ref/tags` endpoint). **GUARD-RAIL (A) — `setLiveAuto` MUST be the ONLY write path for AI auto-detect to reach Live; it MUST NOT call `setSlides` / `setLiveSlideIndex` / `setIsLive(true)` separately**: those three together are the operator-manual go-live trio and they intentionally clear `pinnedPreviewSlide` (the operator's promotion of preview to live is a conscious consumption of the pin). The auto-detect path is the opposite — it's an INVOLUNTARY broadcast that must NEVER touch operator-curated state. Any future refactor that re-routes auto-detect through the manual trio re-introduces the pin-clobber bug that v0.7.203 exists to fix. Audit: `rg -n "setLiveAuto\b" artifacts/imported-app/src/` should show the call inside logos-shell auto-fire effect (and the test file), NOT inside any manual setter. **GUARD-RAIL (B) — `buildOutputPayload` MUST read `s.liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` in that EXACT order (direct ref first, indexed fallback second)**: reversing the order means the manual slides[] would always win even when AI just fired via setLiveAuto, breaking the auto-fire-without-slides-mutation contract. Returning to a sole `slides[liveSlideIndex]` read regresses to v0.7.201/v0.7.202 behaviour. Audit: `rg -n "baseCur|liveSlide" artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/output-payload.ts` MUST show the nullish coalesce with liveSlide on the left. **GUARD-RAIL (C) — `logos-shell.tsx` Live Display column MUST read `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` at BOTH L977 and L1695**: there are two render paths (main column + alt layout) and they MUST agree, otherwise one layout shows the AI's verse and the other shows the stale slides[] verse — operator's monitor and the projector would diverge during a live service. Audit: `rg -n "liveSlide \?\? slides" artifacts/imported-app/src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx | wc -l` MUST return 2. **GUARD-RAIL (D) — Every operator-manual setter that semantically "takes over from AI" MUST clear `liveSlide`**: the list is `setSlides`, `setLiveSlideIndex`, `setIsLive(false)`, `selectScheduleItem`, `clearSchedule`, `removeAllScheduleItems(false)`. Adding a new operator action that promotes a slide to live REQUIRES clearing `liveSlide` in the same set() call — otherwise the AI's direct ref shadows the operator's manual choice and the operator sees their click "do nothing". Audit: `rg -n "liveSlide:\s*null" artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/store.ts | wc -l` MUST be ≥ 6. **GUARD-RAIL (E) — `preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST stay in the default `pnpm test` run and MUST stay at 8 passing tests**: this is the regression net for the operator's most painful recurring bug class. Test 3 (AI fires v25 → pin SURVIVES on v19) is the load-bearing assertion — if it ever fails, the operator's snap-back bug is back. Test file uses a `memStorage` localStorage shim because Zustand persist requires `globalThis.localStorage` to exist; do NOT remove it. Each test uses `useAppStore.getState()` freshly on every read (NOT a captured `const st = useAppStore.getState()` snapshot) because Zustand getState() returns a frozen view of state at that instant — stale snapshots after mutations were the only test failure during development. Audit: `pnpm --filter @workspace/imported-app exec vitest run src/lib/preview-live-isolation.test.ts` MUST report `Tests  8 passed (8)`. **PROCESS GR — When the operator rejects a fix saying "the problem still not solved", DO NOT immediately re-ship a tweaked version. STOP. Verify (1) what build version the operator is actually running (their installed .exe may be 2 releases behind your workspace code), (2) whether the fix in the workspace has ever been compiled+shipped+auto-updated to their machine, (3) whether the operator's described behaviour matches the SHIPPED code or the WORKSPACE code**: this hotfix's stalemate (operator screenshot v0.7.201 behaviour while I had v0.7.203 fix sitting in workspace unshipped) was only broken by explicitly explaining the workspace-vs-shipped gap and offering an automated test as proof BEFORE shipping. The 8-test Vitest suite gave operator confidence to approve the push. Future versions of this same bug class (or any operator-rejected fix) should follow the same pattern: write a failing-on-old-code, passing-on-new-code regression test, run it, show the green output, get explicit approval, THEN ship. **PROCESS GR — When pushing via `.local/push-r.mjs` and the bash tool times out at 60s, DO NOT assume the push failed**: the GitHub REST sequence typically completes in the background even when bash returns -1. Verify with `curl -s -H "Authorization: token $GH_PAT" "https://api.github.com/repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/ref/tags/<tag>"` — if the ref returns with a valid sha, the push succeeded and a retry would just create duplicate refs. This pattern is documented across v0.7.199 and now v0.7.203.

## v0.7.199 — Top-to-down audit hotfix bundle (A+B+C+D+E+F): Unicode NFC normaliser + RTL plumbing + per-locale filler dictionary + SSE heartbeat prune + NDI IPC defensive guards + React ErrorBoundary

-   **v0.7.199 — Top-to-down audit hotfix bundle (A+B+C+D+E+F): Unicode NFC normaliser + RTL plumbing + per-locale filler dictionary + SSE heartbeat prune + NDI IPC defensive guards + React ErrorBoundary**: Operator asked "check through whole app top-to-down, fix bugs, make great for global usage" on v0.7.198. Diagnose pass (typecheck clean + GR audit + explore subagent + manual file inspection) surfaced ~15 candidates; verified two of the explore subagent's "critical" claims were false alarms (#1 NDI ticker starvation — `sendBusy` guard already at `electron/ndi-service.ts` L491; #3 protocol-handler path traversal — guard already at `electron/main.ts` L2783-2785 per v0.7.194-hotfix.12 GR). Presented operator a choice menu; operator chose "BOTH bundles" (global-usage + defensive hardening = 6 items). **Honesty caveat**: items A/B/C are PLUMBING — no CJK/Hebrew/Arabic Bible JSON is currently bundled in `src/data/bibles/` (only kjv/niv/esv/nlt/nkjv/amp/twiasante/ewe — all Latin-script LTR), so A/B/C are infrastructure changes that future-proof the renderer + detection pipeline for the day a non-Latin Bible is added. **Changes (7 files, 171 +ins / 20 -dels)**: (1) **A. Unicode NFC normaliser** — `src/lib/bibles/reference-engine.ts` L95-104 `wordsToNumbers` now prepends `text.normalize('NFC')` before tokenize. Twi (`twiasante`) and Ewe use diacritics (`ɛ`, `ɔ`, `ŋ`, combining tone marks) that speech engines and clipboard paste may emit in NFD form; the bundled book-mapping alias table loads as NFC at bundle time, so without normalisation the same logical word compared unequal at the byte level → alias lookup missed → fuzzy fallback → wrong/no detection. NFC is a no-op on ASCII (zero-cost for English speech path). Architect explicitly validated #6 — safe for English. (2) **B. RTL plumbing** — new file `src/lib/bibles/translation-direction.ts` exports `getTranslationDirection(translation): 'ltr'|'rtl'` + `getDirAttribute(translation): 'rtl'|undefined`. Uses an `RTL_TRANSLATION_IDS` Set + `RTL_TRANSLATION_PREFIXES` array (heb/arabic/aramaic/syriac/farsi/persian/urdu). Every currently-bundled translation returns `'ltr'` → `getDirAttribute` returns `undefined` → React skips emitting the attr → rendered DOM is byte-identical to pre-v0.7.199. Applied to `src/components/presenter/slide-renderer.tsx` L381 (verse container reads `useAppStore.getState().selectedTranslation` — first attempt used `.translation` which doesn't exist on `AppState`, typecheck caught it). NOT applied to `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` in this hotfix — that path renders inside the outer template literal where v0.7.196 GR-D (no extra backticks in comments) and v0.7.198 GR-C (cache-key shape parity across L1199/L1490/L1562) make every render-gate edit a 3-site lockstep change. RTL on the secondary surface is a follow-up if a non-LTR Bible is bundled; until then no behavioural difference because there's no RTL Bible to render. (3) **C. Per-locale filler dictionary** — `reference-engine.ts` L169-231 restructured `FILLER_PHRASES` array → `FILLER_BY_LOCALE: Record<string, string[]>` keyed by ISO 2-letter code. English bucket (`en`) carries the existing 20 phrases unchanged. `tw` + `ee` buckets are empty stubs (native-speaker contributor populates later). `stripFiller` got an optional second arg `locale?: string`; precedence is locale-bucket-first then always-also-English (bilingual services mix English Bible vocab into non-English preaching). Legacy `FILLER_PHRASES` const kept as alias pointing at `FILLER_BY_LOCALE.en` so existing single-arg callsites are byte-identical (verified by architect #3). (4) **D. SSE heartbeat prune** — `src/lib/output-broadcast.ts` L146-192 added 25-second heartbeat interval emitting SSE comment line `:hb\n\n` to every subscriber. Comment lines are valid SSE per spec — browser EventSource drops them silently. The Node `res.write` attempt eventually returns false (or throws) on truly-dead sockets, the existing prune path catches it, and the subscriber Set stays bounded. Pre-fix the only prune trigger was a real state broadcast; stalled-but-not-closed sockets (Wi-Fi blip, suspended laptop, dead TV that never sent FIN) silently buffered in the OS socket buffer for up to ~50k broadcasts before write returned false → during a long live service, dead subscribers stacked in the Set and every real update walked all of them. 25s is well under common HTTP idle-timeout (60s ALB, 100s Cloudflare) so this doubles as a keep-alive through future reverse proxies. Idempotency: `globalThis.__scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat` singleton guard prevents stacking across Next.js hot reload (architect explicitly validated #2). NOT unref'd — the SSE broadcaster is the heart of the live service, keeping the event loop alive on its behalf is the right tradeoff. (5) **E. NDI IPC defensive guards** — `electron/main.ts` L2250-2266 `ndi:status` wrapped in try/catch returning structured `NdiStatus`-shaped fallback `{running:false, frameCount:0, error: ndi.unavailableReason()||message}` on throw. Pre-fix if the bundled DLL failed to load (corrupt install / missing VC++ runtime / blocked by AV) and any internal field on `NdiService` was nullable, the IPC call surfaced as `invoke` rejection with no diagnostic. L2432-2434 `ndi:stop` got `if (!ndi.isAvailable()) return {ok:true, alreadyStopped:true}` matching the existing `ndi:start` guard at L2241. Architect's pass 1 caught the initial fallback shape used invented fields (`sourceName`/`layout`/`transparent`/`lowerThird`/`available`/`unavailableReason`) that don't exist on the `NdiStatus` type at `electron/ndi-service.ts` L29 + `electron/preload.ts` L26 + `src/lib/use-electron.ts` L5; rewrote to use only the three real fields. Renderer reads NDI availability via the separate `appInfo.ndiAvailable` channel (preload.ts L42) so the status object only needs `running` + `frameCount` + `error`. (6) **F. React ErrorBoundary** — new file `src/components/error-boundary.tsx` is a class component with `static getDerivedStateFromError` + `componentDidCatch` (logs to console for DevTools + Electron launch.log capture), renders a recoverable fallback card with Reload button on catch. `src/app/page.tsx` wraps `<LogosShell>` at L74-76 (label="Live Console") and `<SettingsView>` at L100-102 (label="Settings"). Boundary placement is deliberate: providers (`SpeechProvider`, `OutputBroadcaster`, `LicenseProvider`, `LiveTranslationSync`, `UpdateNotifier`) stay OUTSIDE the boundary so when a deep child of LogosShell crashes mid-service (corrupt cache row, malformed slide shape, missing media asset), the SSE broadcaster + NDI sender + license heartbeat keep running — the secondary screen + NDI feed keep broadcasting whatever was last on-air, the operator console shows the recoverable card, and clicking Reload re-mounts only the LogosShell subtree. Architect validated #4. NOT wrapped per-card inside `logos-shell.tsx` (4199 lines) — nested boundaries inside that file would require re-validating every v0.7.197 GR-B preview-lock site, large risk for cosmetic gain. (7) `package.json` 0.7.198→0.7.199 + `BUILD.bat` banner. **Architect pre-push reviewed and rejected first attempt (#5 blocker — ndi:status fallback shape mismatch); after the rewrite to use the real `NdiStatus` field set, architect APPROVED** with all 8 validations passing: GR-respect, heartbeat singleton, stripFiller back-compat, ErrorBoundary placement preserving broadcast continuity, NdiStatus shape parity, NFC ASCII no-op, all-LTR defaults, undefined-dir React no-op. Shipped commit `0eb769a` tag `v0.7.199` via `.local/push-r.mjs` (first invocation timed out at the bash-tool 60s wall clock, but the GitHub REST sequence had already completed — HEAD on GitHub was already `0eb769a` with the v0.7.199 message when verified). **GUARD-RAIL (A) — `wordsToNumbers` MUST keep `text.normalize('NFC')` at the entry**: the book-mapping alias table is loaded as NFC at bundle time. Removing the normaliser re-introduces a class of detection misses where the same logical word (e.g. Twi "ɛyɛ") compares unequal at the byte level between speech-engine output and the alias table → alias lookup fails → fuzzy fallback degrades or returns wrong book. Cost is sub-µs on the speech chunks the engine actually sees; benefit is correctness across every non-ASCII translation we may add. Audit: `rg -n "normalize\(.NFC.\)" src/lib/bibles/reference-engine.ts` MUST show the call at the top of `wordsToNumbers`. **GUARD-RAIL (B) — `getDirAttribute` MUST return `undefined` (not `'ltr'`) for LTR translations**: React skips emitting an attribute whose value is `undefined`, so the DOM stays byte-identical for every currently-bundled translation. Returning the string `'ltr'` would add `dir="ltr"` everywhere → snapshot tests churn + downstream CSS that uses `[dir]` attribute selectors would suddenly match LTR roots. The two-helper split (`getTranslationDirection` returns the canonical 'ltr'|'rtl' for logic; `getDirAttribute` returns undefined|'rtl' for JSX spread) is load-bearing — do NOT collapse them. Audit: `rg -n "return 'ltr'|return undefined" src/lib/bibles/translation-direction.ts` MUST show `getDirAttribute` returning `undefined` on the LTR branch, NOT the string `'ltr'`. **GUARD-RAIL (C) — `stripFiller` MUST keep the optional `locale?: string` second arg AND MUST always also run the English bucket regardless of locale**: bilingual services routinely mix English Bible vocabulary ("read", "turn to", "word of god") into non-English preaching — dropping English when a non-English locale is passed would regress detection on the majority of real-world bilingual usage. The legacy single-arg call form MUST stay byte-identical to pre-v0.7.199: `stripFiller(text)` runs English-only (locale undefined → first branch skipped → second branch runs English bucket = exact pre-v0.7.199 behaviour). Audit: `rg -n "stripFiller\(" src/lib/bibles/` and every callsite MUST either pass no locale (back-compat) or pass an explicit locale; never gate the English bucket on locale being undefined. **GUARD-RAIL (D) — `output-broadcast.ts` heartbeat interval MUST use the `globalThis.__scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat` singleton guard**: without it, every Next.js hot-reload during dev (and every fresh module-graph evaluation in some Edge runtime scenarios) stacks another 25-second interval → after 10 hot-reloads, 10 heartbeats fire concurrently → 10× the prune work + 10× the keep-alive traffic. The `declare global { var __scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat: NodeJS.Timeout | undefined }` + `if (!globalThis.__scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat)` pattern is the standard Next.js singleton — do NOT replace it with a module-level `let` (module-level state resets on hot-reload, defeating the guard). Heartbeat MUST also stay NOT-unref'd; the SSE broadcaster is the heart of the live service, keeping the event loop alive on its behalf is intentional. Audit: `rg -n "__scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat" src/lib/output-broadcast.ts` MUST show the `declare global` + `if (!globalThis.__scriptureLiveSseHeartbeat)` pair. **GUARD-RAIL (E) — `ipcMain.handle('ndi:status')` catch-path fallback object MUST keep the exact `NdiStatus` shape `{running:boolean, frameCount:number, error?:string}`** (plus optional `source/width/height/fps/captureMessage` only if added to the type later). Inventing fields the renderer doesn't read is harmless but inventing fields that conflict with the type (`sourceName` vs the real `source`) breaks TS consumers + can silently overwrite real fields when the renderer does `{...status, …}` spread. The renderer reads NDI availability via the separate `appInfo.ndiAvailable` channel (`electron/preload.ts` L42), so the status fallback does NOT need to invent an `available` field. Three files define `NdiStatus` (`electron/ndi-service.ts` L29 + `electron/preload.ts` L26 + `src/lib/use-electron.ts` L5) and MUST stay in sync — when any one changes, the other two MUST be updated in the same commit. Audit: `rg -n "type NdiStatus" electron/ src/lib/use-electron.ts` MUST show all three definitions with identical field sets. **GUARD-RAIL (F) — `ErrorBoundary` placement in `page.tsx` MUST wrap ONLY `LogosShell` and `SettingsView` (NOT the providers above them)**: SpeechProvider, OutputBroadcaster, LiveTranslationSync, UpdateNotifier, LicenseProvider all live OUTSIDE the boundary so when the LogosShell subtree crashes mid-broadcast, the SSE broadcaster keeps emitting to the secondary screen, the NDI sender keeps emitting frames to OBS/vMix, the license heartbeat keeps the subscription state valid, and the speech recognition stays hot. Operator clicks Reload to recover the console subtree only. Wrapping providers inside the boundary would mean a console crash also tears down SSE/NDI mid-service — the exact failure mode this hotfix exists to prevent. Audit: `rg -nB1 -A2 "<ErrorBoundary" src/app/page.tsx` MUST show both ErrorBoundary instances sitting BELOW `<LicenseProvider>` and `<SpeechProvider>` in the JSX tree, never above. **PROCESS GR — When the diagnose pass surfaces a "critical" claim from an explore subagent, VERIFY by reading the actual file at the claimed line before accepting it**: this hotfix had two such false alarms (NDI ticker starvation + protocol-handler path traversal) that the subagent confidently labelled critical. Both were already guarded by code the subagent didn't read. Cost of verification: 2 grep + 2 sed commands, ~30 seconds. Cost of trusting the subagent: a wasted hotfix entry "fixing" a non-bug, plus the cost of operator confusion when the GR audit later shows the guard was already there. **PROCESS GR — When a hotfix bundle has N items, the architect pre-push MUST be passed explicit per-item validations (#1..#N) not a generic "review the diff"**: this hotfix's architect prompt enumerated 8 validations and the architect's reply mapped 1:1 to each → the one blocker (NdiStatus shape mismatch) was caught precisely because validation #5 specifically asked about it. A generic "review the diff" review would likely have missed the shape mismatch among 171 insertions across 7 files.

## v0.7.198 — NDI Live Preview flash fix (iframe key stabilized) + settings preview boxes show background only (`?noMedia=1` query gate) + NDI-in-OBS frame-flow diagnostic logs

-   **v0.7.198 — NDI Live Preview flash fix (iframe key stabilized) + settings preview boxes show background only (`?noMedia=1` query gate) + NDI-in-OBS frame-flow diagnostic logs**: Operator reported via screenshots: (1) NDI Live Preview iframe flashes/reloads every time they flip Full↔LT mode in NDI panel — visible flicker on operator's monitor, distracting during live service. (2) Settings page preview boxes (in Display Output settings AND NDI panel preview) show full video media including looping mp4 playback — operator wants those preview thumbnails to show ONLY background/theme so they don't churn decoder load while operator is tweaking settings; Main Preview/Live + offscreen FrameCapture + Browser Source must still play media. (3) NDI source appears in OBS source dropdown on same PC but renders BLACK — unknown when broke, need diagnostics before guessing root cause. **Changes (5 files)**: (1) `src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` L1467 `stableKey` changed from a composite key including `ndiDisplayMode` + `position` to a constant string `'ndi-preview'` — the iframe URL itself already carries the mode params and the renderer reads live mode via SSE (per hotfix.5 GR), so React key only needs to be stable across the panel lifetime. Pre-fix, every flip of `ndiDisplayMode` changed the key → React unmounted+remounted the iframe → operator saw a black flash + reload spinner. L1430 also added `&noMedia=1` to the preview URL params (preview thumbnail should not play media, see item 2). (2) `src/components/settings/output-preview.tsx` — added optional `noMedia?: boolean` prop; when true, appends `&noMedia=1` to the iframe src. (3) `src/components/views/settings.tsx` L961 + L1458 — passed `noMedia={true}` on the two settings-page OutputPreview instances (Display Output settings preview + NDI panel preview). Main Preview/Live columns in `logos-shell.tsx` + offscreen FrameCapture BrowserWindow + Browser Source HTTP route consumers do NOT pass `noMedia` → unchanged → videos play as before. (4) `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` — `var IS_NO_MEDIA=false;` injected at L287 (inside outer html template literal), parsed from URL at L330 (`IS_NO_MEDIA = u.searchParams.get('noMedia')==='1'`), and gated at the slide-render entry L1466 with an explicit early-bail branch: for any `media`-type slide when `IS_NO_MEDIA` is true, render ONLY the themed background card (`setBgVid(safeBgM)` + theme card + bg-overlay) and `return` BEFORE the media element render path. Architect pass 1 caught the original implementation falling through to text-render of `slide.title` (which is the upload filename — "video.mp4") so the early-bail had to be explicit. Architect pass 2 caught a cache-key shape mismatch — the render-entry key at L1199 had to gain a `nm:IS_NO_MEDIA?1:0` field to match the new branch writes at L1490 (early-bail) and L1562 (media-reuse); pre-fix the noMedia and noMedia=0 surfaces would have collided in the cache slot, causing the first-rendered surface to block the other's rebuild. All three lastRenderKey writes are now shape-identical. (5) `electron/frame-capture.ts` — added frame-flow diagnostic logging for NDI-in-OBS BLACK investigation: `[frame-capture] subscription started` on `start()` + per-60-frame `frames=N fps=X.X black=Y.Y% size=WxH` rate-limited stats. Blackness check samples 8 pixels per frame from the BGRA `Uint8ClampedArray` (`buf[i]+buf[i+1]+buf[i+2]>24` threshold per sample) and tracks the rolling percentage. Goal: when operator reproduces the BLACK in OBS, the launch.log shows whether (a) `[frame-capture] subscription started` ever fired (subscription broken) OR (b) frames=0 (no frames being captured) OR (c) frames>0 with black=100% (capture working but sending all-black pixels — points to upstream BrowserWindow render issue) OR (d) frames>0 with black=0% (NDI sender working, problem is in OBS receiver config). Logs are zero-cost when subscription is healthy (60-frame interval at 30fps = 1 line every 2 seconds). `package.json` 0.7.197→0.7.198, `BUILD.bat` banner bumped. `pnpm --filter @workspace/imported-app exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Architect pass 3 GREEN: cache-key shape parity across entry + 2 branch writes confirmed; `IS_NO_MEDIA` consumer enumeration shows only the 3 expected sites; v0.7.196 GR-D backtick check clean (initially violated when I put backticks around `nm` in a comment inside the outer template literal, tsc caught it immediately at L1189 col 20, fixed by removing the backticks). Pushed commit `af244b5` tag `v0.7.198` via `.local/push-r.mjs`. **GUARD-RAIL (A) — NDI Live Preview iframe `stableKey` MUST stay a constant string (`'ndi-preview'`), NEVER include `ndiDisplayMode` / `ndiFullScreenBackground` / `position` / `wantTransparent` as key fields**: the iframe URL params already carry mode info, and the renderer adapts to mode flips via the SSE channel (per hotfix.5 GR) — putting any mode-derived value in the React key re-introduces the flash-on-flip operator bug (unmount + remount + black frame + reload spinner during live service). The iframe `src` URL is allowed (and required) to change with mode; React updates the iframe's src in-place without unmounting when the key is stable. Audit: `rg -n "stableKey" src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` MUST show a constant string literal, NOT a template literal interpolating mode/position/transparent fields. **GUARD-RAIL (B) — `IS_NO_MEDIA` early-bail at `route.ts` L1466 MUST be an explicit `return` BEFORE the media element render path**: pre-fix the original implementation let media slides fall through to a text-render branch that displayed `slide.title` — which for operator-uploaded media is the literal filename ("congregation-walk.mp4"). The early-bail MUST render ONLY the themed background card (`setBgVid(safeBgM)` + theme card + bg-overlay) and `return` so neither the media element NOR the text-render branch ever executes. Audit: `rg -n "IS_NO_MEDIA" src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` MUST show (i) declaration at L287, (ii) URL parse at L330, (iii) slide-render entry gate at L1466 with explicit `return` after the bg-only render, AND (iv) cache-key `nm` field at L1199/L1490/L1562 — exactly 4 sites; any extra site is a smell. **GUARD-RAIL (C) — `lastRenderKey` JSON shape MUST stay identical across the render-entry write (L1199) and ALL branch writes (L1490 early-bail, L1562 media-reuse)**: any field added to one site MUST be added to all three with the SAME expression form (currently `nm:IS_NO_MEDIA?1:0` in all three). Shape divergence causes cache poisoning where the noMedia and noMedia=0 surfaces collide on the same slot and whichever rendered first blocks the other's rebuild (operator sees blank surface). Audit: `rg -n "lastRenderKey\s*=" src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` should show exactly 3 write sites all using the same `JSON.stringify({sl,dm,st,ndi,nm})` shape. **GUARD-RAIL (D) — Settings-page preview thumbnails MUST pass `noMedia={true}` to OutputPreview**: this is the contract that lets operator tweak settings without churning the video decoder pool. Main Preview/Live columns in logos-shell.tsx + offscreen FrameCapture BrowserWindow + Browser Source HTTP route consumers MUST NOT pass `noMedia` (or pass `false`) → videos play as before. Adding `noMedia` to a non-settings consumer would break operator's live-broadcast media playback. Audit: `rg -n "noMedia" src/components/settings/ src/components/views/settings.tsx src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` — every match in the settings tree should be `true`; logos-shell.tsx + frame-capture.ts + main.ts paths MUST NOT mention `noMedia`. **GUARD-RAIL (E) — `frame-capture.ts` per-60-frame stats log line MUST stay in place across cleanups; it is the diagnostic canary for the NDI-in-OBS BLACK class of bug**: at 30fps a 60-frame interval = 1 log line per 2 seconds = zero noticeable flood, and the 8-pixel blackness sample is O(8) per frame = negligible. When operator reports "NDI source dropdown shows ScriptureLive AI but render is black in OBS/vMix/Wirecast," the launch.log line `frames=N fps=X.X black=Y.Y% size=WxH` immediately distinguishes 4 root-cause classes: (a) no log = subscription never started, (b) frames=0 = subscription started but no callback firing, (c) frames>0 black=100% = NDI sending all-black pixels (upstream BrowserWindow render issue), (d) frames>0 black<10% = NDI sending real pixels, problem is OBS-side receiver config. Removing these logs forces guess-driven debug cycles. Audit: `rg -n "\[frame-capture\]" electron/frame-capture.ts` MUST show both `subscription started` and the per-60-frame stats log. **PROCESS GR — When adding a new URL query param that gates render branches inside the outer `const html = \`…\`` template literal in congregation/route.ts, the param parse + injection + every branch-write that exists in the cache-key system MUST be updated in lockstep**: this hotfix's pass 2 caught the L1199 entry-key not matching the L1490/L1562 branch writes — easy bug class because there are 3 write sites scattered across 400 lines of inline JS. Any future "add a render gate" change MUST grep `rg -n "lastRenderKey\s*=" route.ts` BEFORE shipping and verify ALL writes match the entry shape. **PROCESS GR — v0.7.196 GUARD-RAIL (D) violations are catchable by `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` and surface as cryptic comma-expected errors at the line where the stray backtick appears**: I violated it once this hotfix by putting backticks around `nm` in a comment. tsc reported `route.ts(1189,20): ',' expected` which is the textbook signature of template-literal-terminated-mid-file. When you see that error class inside congregation/route.ts, IMMEDIATELY grep `rg -n '\\\\\`|` src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` and remove any non-intended backtick before chasing other hypotheses.

## v0.7.197 — Bible-text autofit overflow fix on ALL secondary surfaces (`.slide-content` had no height bound) + snap-back preview clobber fix (6th AI-push site missed by hotfix.11 — auto-live useEffect in logos-shell.tsx)

-   **v0.7.197 — Bible-text autofit overflow fix on ALL secondary surfaces (`.slide-content` had no height bound) + snap-back preview clobber fix (6th AI-push site missed by hotfix.11 — auto-live useEffect in logos-shell.tsx)**: Operator on v0.7.196 reported via 5 screenshots (2026-05-17): main app PREVIEW + LIVE columns render verse correctly, but Typography preview, Display & Output Live Preview, NDI Live Preview, and Wirecast/OBS ALL show massively oversized Bible text clipped top AND bottom. "Text Size on Secondary Screen" already at 60% and still overflowing. Said "fix the work you did right" — pointing at autofit. **Root cause (Item 1)**: `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L74 `.slide-content` CSS was `position:relative;z-index:1;text-align:center;width:90%;max-width:90vw;padding:4vh 3vw;display:flex;flex-direction:column;align-items:center;justify-content:center` — NO height/max-height/overflow constraint. It is a flex child of `#output` (`display:flex;align-items:center`) so without an explicit height it sizes to content. `fitVerseText` at L946-982 computes `var avail=parent.clientHeight` then binary-searches the largest scale `best` where `h<=avail*safety && w<=availW`. With unbounded parent, `parent.clientHeight` reports content-height (always grows with the text itself) → `h<=avail` always passes → `best=maxK=1.00` → text rendered at full baseSize (~64px in 1920×1080 NDI/preview iframes via clamp `min(7cqw,12cqh)` resolution) → overflow top+bottom. Main app PREVIEW/LIVE columns escaped the bug because their tiny preview iframes (~150px tall) floor the clamp() baseSize at the 16px rem-minimum so even at scale 1.0 the text fits. LT branch (`.lt-content` L171) already had `height:100%;overflow:hidden;min-height:0` so was unaffected — only the FULLSCREEN branch was broken. **Root cause (Item 2)**: operator reported a separate "snap-back" bug: single-click verse 19 (intending preview=19, live=18) → preview flashes 19 then snaps back to 18. Hotfix.11 added preview-lock guards to 5 speech-provider AI-push sites but missed the SIXTH AI-push site at `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L3875 — an auto-live useEffect that fires from the `addDetectedVerse` pipeline (mic hot, AI hears preacher quoting "John 3:18" once per second).
